70.

...concerning the two persons, in which he clearly portrays them with their two kingdoms, as if through himself, without any external commentary <note>The original German "ohne Gloß" refers to "glosses," which were marginal notes or interpretations added by scholars; the author argues the text is self-evident.</note>. For now, the words within are pure spirit and life, just as everything lives and moves outside in heaven and on earth. Both of these kingdoms are also embodied in the book, including everything they possess in Jesus Christ. They are thus enclosed within his worthy <term>humanity</term> <note>original: "Menschheit"; here referring to the physical and historical existence of Christ as the vessel for the divine.</note>—both spiritually and bodily—nothing being excluded that does not find its <term>final fulfillment</term> <note>original: "Endtschafft"; the ultimate goal or completion of a process.</note> in him.

Therefore, we ought rightly to rejoice and be glad in God, being thankful for such grace that he shows us in his beloved Son. Through this, the Fatherly essence is so richly revealed to us poor little creatures, without any interference of <term>human delusion</term> <note>original: "Menschlichem wahn"; refers to the flawed or prideful ideas of men as opposed to divine truth.</note>. By this, we are forever certainly assured in Christ. To him be honor and praise from eternity to eternity, Amen.

->In the year 1545.<-

This reflects the early 17th-century interest in "Theosophy," which blended art, music, and theology.</meta>

### The Revelation of Jesus Christ:
->That is:<-

# A Proof by way of the
## Title above the Cross of Jesus Christ,
and the three alphabets—namely Hebrew, Greek,
and Latin—as well as several
wonderful figures. <note>In this context, "figures" refers to the intricate, symbolic diagrams Lautensack designed to illustrate the hidden workings of the divine.</note>

In what manner the one God revealed himself in various
ways and methods, and finally, without any figure,
truly and perfectly in the person of Jesus Christ.

By the godly Paul Lautensack,
painter and organist formerly of Nuremberg. To
which, for the sake of fuller understanding, the
interpretation of Master Valentin Weigel has been
added.

Wherein is to be found how <term>Man</term> <note>original: "Mensch"; here referring to the collective human soul or the microcosm.</note> moves in one harmony <note>original: "Thon," literally "tone" or "pitch."</note> with God, heaven,
and earth, through the Word—which at the end
of the world became flesh—and how the
Devil's <term>dissonance</term> <note>original: "dissonans"; a musical metaphor likely chosen because Lautensack was a professional organist. It suggests that evil is a disruption of the natural, divine melody of creation.</note>
is prevented.

...concerning the two persons, <note>Likely referring to the relationship between the Divine and Human natures of Christ, or the relationship between God and the individual soul.</note> wherein He clearly depicts them with their two kingdoms—as if through Himself, without any gloss—so that henceforth the words within are pure spirit and life, just as everything lives and moves externally in heaven and on earth. These two kingdoms are also incorporated into this book, with everything they possess in Jesus Christ, <note>original: "Jesu Christo"</note> thus enclosed within His worthy humanity, <term>humanity</term> <note>original: "Menschheit"; in this mystical context, it refers to the state of being human as a vessel for the divine.</note> both spiritually and physically, with nothing excepted that does not find its ultimate fulfillment <note>original: "Endtschafft," implying a final goal or consummation.</note> in Him.

Therefore, we should rightly rejoice and be glad in God, being thankful for such grace as He has shown us in His dear Son; for the Fatherly essence is thus abundantly revealed to us poor little creatures, <note>original: "Creatürlein"; the use of the diminutive suggests a tone of profound humility before the Creator.</note> without the interference of human delusion, through which we are forever certainly assured in Christ. To Him be honor and praise from eternity to eternity, Amen.

## Revelation of Jesus Christ:
### That is:

# A Proof through the Title above the Cross of Jesus Christ,
->and the three alphabets, namely Hebrew, Greek, and Latin, as well as several wondrous figures.<- <note>original: "Hebräisch/ Grægisch/ vnd Lateinisch"; this refers to the "Titulus" or the INRI inscription placed above Jesus during the crucifixion, which was written in these three languages.</note>

->In what manner the one God, in various ways and modes, and finally without any <term>figure</term> <note>In this context, "figure" refers to a symbolic representation or shadow, contrasted with the "truth" and "perfection" found in the person of Christ.</note> has truly and perfectly revealed Himself in the person of Jesus Christ.<-

->By the pious Paul Lautensack, formerly a painter and organist in Nuremberg. For the sake of a fuller understanding, the commentary of Master Valentin Weigel has been added.<- <note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588) was a prominent Lutheran pastor and mystic whose radical ideas on inner spirituality were published mostly after his death.</note>

->Wherein is to be found how man, with God, Heaven, and Earth, moves in one harmony through the Word—which at the end of the world became flesh—and how the devil’s dissonance may be prevented.<- <note>The author uses musical terminology ("one tone" and "dissonance") to describe the spiritual alignment between the Creator and the soul.</note>

## Preface to the Reader.

According to Jeremiah 9, the wise man should not boast of his wisdom, nor the strong man of his strength, nor the rich man of his riches; rather, he who wishes to boast should boast of this: that he understands and knows the Lord.

Although everyone twists and bends these heart-felt sayings, as well as others, according to their own whim <note>literally: "according to their own brain"</note>, in such a way that one cannot sufficiently exalt himself over another in boasting of such wholesome knowledge: therefore these voices ring out everywhere: "Here is the temple of the Lord!" "We have Moses and the Prophets!" "We are from our father Abraham!" "We stand in the <term>line of succession from Peter</term> <note>original: "Linea successione Petri"; the theological claim that a church's authority is derived through a direct chain of bishops stretching back to the Apostle Peter</note>." 

Even though—I say—such and similar <term>shouts of acclaim</term> <note>original: "acclamationes"</note> and <term>protests</term> <note>original: "reclamationes"</note>—arranged like a three-part <term>musical choir</term> <note>original: "Musici chori"</note>—resound and are driven throughout the whole world beneath the spherical clouds of heaven <term>to the point of hoarseness for many</term> <note>original: "usque ad raucedinem multorum"</note>, yet to the sensible man, when he with his eyes

<meta>catchword: of the</meta>

<summary>This preface opens with a biblical citation from Jeremiah to humble the reader, before critiquing how various religious factions claim exclusive authority and divine favor. The author suggests that despite the loud, competing claims of different churches, a sensible person must look deeper.</summary>

# Preface to the Reader.

It should be (Jeremiah 9) that neither the wise man boasts of his wisdom, nor the strong man of his strength, nor the rich man of his riches; rather, he who wishes to boast should boast of this: that he understands and knows the Lord.

Although everyone now twists and turns these heartfelt sayings—as they do others—according to their own whims, such that one person cannot sufficiently exalt themselves over another in boasting of such soul-saving knowledge: hence these voices ring out on all sides: "Here is the Temple of the Lord!" "We have Moses and the Prophets!" "We are descended from our father Abraham!" "We stand in the <term>line of succession from Peter<note>original Latin: Linea successione Petri; the Catholic doctrine that authority is passed down directly from the Apostle Peter.</note></term>!" Although, I say, such and similar <term>shouts of praise<note>original Latin: acclamationes</note></term> and <term>cries of protest<note>original Latin: reclamationes</note></term>—performed like a three-part commissioned <term>musical choir<note>original Latin: Musici chori</note></term>—resound and are driven throughout the whole world beneath the spherical clouds of heaven <term>until many are hoarse<note>original Latin: usque ad raucedinem multorum</note></term>, yet to the discerning man, when he [looks] with his eyes

<summary>The author uses Jeremiah 9 to argue that true pride should only be found in knowing God, critiquing how various religious factions loudly claim exclusive authority through Moses, Abraham, or the lineage of Peter. This page serves as a polemic against sectarian arrogance and the performative nature of religious disputes.</summary>
<keywords>Jeremiah 9, humility, religious authority, Petrine succession, sectarianism, theology, musical metaphor</keywords>

# Preface

of the mind only a little views the matter, and
will pay attention with the ears of the heart, it will
be easily discerned that these are musical signs or
*notes without words, sermons without a subject* <note>original Latin: *notæ sine verbis, prædicationes sine subiecto*</note>, and a
*bellowing* <note>original Latin: *boatus*; often used by Reformers to describe liturgical chanting they considered mindless or animal-like</note>, intended only to fill the carnal ears of the
unlearned. For who among them all walks in the
Light, while all their works are evil?
And since they loved the darkness more than the
Light, how can they boast that they know God, who
is an unapproachable Light? Especially
when one [considers] the manifold, countless, and excessive
sins, such as immeasurable pride, infinite
greed, superfluous gluttony and drunkenness, cursing
and swearing, unheard-of lust, and shameful
fornication—indeed, <term>sodomitic shame<note>A common early modern term for any sexual behavior considered "unnatural" or non-procreative.</note></term>—and other
vices, which in various lands, provinces,
and kingdoms are practiced as a common trade—
to say nothing of these, about which the holy Apostle
concludes for the second time that those who do such things
will not inherit the kingdom of heaven—and only the
single verse of 1 John 2: "The darkness has passed
away, and the true Light now shines; whoever
says he is in the Light, and hates his brother,
is still in darkness, and knows not where he
goes, for the darkness has blinded his eyes."

# To the Reader.
<sig>)( iij</sig>

...ed. If now, I say, this single verse is rightly taken into account and taken to heart, it is immediately clear as day that they have seen nor known neither God nor His **Anointed One** <note>original: *Gesalbten*; a translation of the Hebrew 'Messiah' or Greek 'Christos'</note>. For right now the very time is at hand of which Christ prophesied that love would grow cold, and therefore no faith would be found at His coming. For 1 John 2: *He who says "I know Him," and yet does not keep His commandment, is a liar, and there is no truth in him*, and (1 John 1) *if we say we have fellowship with Him and walk in darkness, we lie and do not practice the truth*: therefore the aforementioned Apostle in his 1st Epistle, Chapter 3, concludes: *whoever sins has not seen nor known Him*, for which reason also our faith is dead, our boasting false, and our hope in vain. For how shall we still be found in sins, we who seek to be made righteous through Christ? Is Christ then a <term>servant of sin<note>original: *Sündendiener*; a reference to Galatians 2:17. The author is arguing against "cheap grace," or the idea that one can be a Christian while continuing to live a sinful life.</note></term>? According to Galatians 2, has He not come to destroy the works of the devil, so that from now on we should live for righteousness, godliness, and piety? Therefore the Lord God wills, in both the New and Old Testaments, that we should be merciful, righteous, and holy, because He is merciful, righteous, and holy. Therefore, it is only

He emphasizes that Christ came to destroy the works of the devil and that believers are called to mirror God's own holiness and mercy.</summary>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Preface to the Reader." # Preface

Christ is our Way in which we should walk; He is the Truth which should always be found in us; He is our Life. How then can we extend our members <note>original: *Gliedmassen*; refers to the physical body and its faculties being used as "instruments" of sin or righteousness.</note> toward unrighteousness, which is death? Therefore, no one comes to the Father except through Christ; nor does anyone know the Father except the Son, and him to whom the Son wills to reveal Him.

To come to the Father through Christ and to know Him is nothing other than to have a free, detached, and God-surrendered will—claiming neither this nor that for oneself, holding pleasure and pain as one and the same, and thinking, speaking, doing, and knowing nothing but what is fitting for God, in the form and manner of Christ *Jesus*, the firstborn among the children of God.

Regarding this, see the little book called the *German Theology* <note>original: *Teutsche Theologia*; an influential 14th-century mystical treatise that emphasizes the imitation of Christ and the abandonment of self-will. Martin Luther was a famous admirer of this work.</note>, especially in the final *chapters* regarding the saying in *John 6* <note>While the author cites John 6, the quote "No one comes to the Father except through me" is found in John 14:6.</note>: *No one comes to the Father except through Me.* Therefore He says: "Whoever wishes to be My disciple, let him deny himself and follow Me." Those, however, who follow Him as His faithful disciples have eternal life, for they recognize in Christ the Father as a true **GOD**, and through the Holy Spirit recognize Him whom the Father has sent, *Jesus Christ*.

Therefore all boasting should [be cast] far

<summary>The author defines the true knowledge of God as a practical imitation of Christ’s self-surrender rather than a theoretical belief. He cites the "Theologia Germanica" to argue that coming to the Father requires a complete abandonment of personal will and the pursuit of a life "fitting for God."</summary>
<keywords>Jesus Christ, Theologia Germanica, self-denial, imitation of Christ, self-will, Holy Spirit, John 14:6, mystical union</keywords>

# To the Reader.

...should be ours; except alone in the Cross of our Lord
Jesus Christ. For although this is the sign that shall be
spoken against <note>original: *Zeichen, welchem widersprochen würdt*; a reference to Luke 2:34, where Simeon prophesies that Christ is a sign that will be met with opposition.</note>, yet Christ is to be found neither
in the water nor on land, nor in the desert, nor in
royal palaces, nor here or there, but
only on the central Cross. There, He stretches out His arms
to receive the poor and all repentant sinners,
and to gather them with Himself upon the Cross. There,
through His blood, having found eternal redemption <note>original: *nach erfundener ewiger Erlösung*; referring to the completion of the atonement.</note>,
He entered into the Holiest of Holies as the true High
Priest. There, He revealed Himself as the Book of
Life—He who is God and Man, the Alpha and Omega <note>original: *A. vnd O.*; the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing God's eternity as the beginning and the end.</note>.
There, He poured out the streams of living water
which bring salvation to those they reach.
Therefore, even though many shout, sing, and
preach about Christ, and constantly cry out "LORD,
LORD," the Crucified remains unknown,
and the will of the Father is left undone <note>original: *will deß Vatters vnderlassen*; meaning the active, internal obedience to God's will is neglected.</note>, because 
few follow the Mother of Jesus to the Cross.
Instead, many lock their doors out of fear, while
the majority give counsel that Christ should be 
rooted out from among the living.
Since, then, the God-fearing Paulus Lautensack <note>Paulus Lautensack (1478–1558) was a German painter and visionary whose work focused on apocalyptic and mystical themes.</note>
noticed and saw this eighty-one years ago, and 
afterwards the worthy Valentin Weigel <note>original: *M. V. VVeigelius*; Valentin Weigel (1533–1588) was an influential German theologian and mystic whose writings emphasized internal spirituality over outward religious ritual.</note> saw 
that the whole world lies in wickedness and...

# Preface

...it is falsely and antichristianly believed that the Kingdom of God consists in words, whereas it wants to be found and felt in power <note>original: *in der Krafft*; a reference to 1 Corinthians 4:20, "For the kingdom of God is not in word, but in power."</note>. It appears to be bound to many external places, even though such a treasure must be dug up and brought forth from within us, as if from weak vessels <note>original: *schwachen Gefässen*; a reference to 2 Corinthians 4:7, "But we have this treasure in earthen vessels."</note>.

Therefore, first of all, **Paulus Lautensack** <note>Lautensack (1478–1558) was a German painter and organist known for his complex mystical and apocalyptic diagrams.</note>, not without the special illumination of the Holy Spirit, composed several small treatises on the **Revelation of Jesus Christ**, both in writings and in figures <note>original: *Figuren*; referring to the intricate mystical illustrations or diagrams common in his work.</note>. He calls this book (namely, the Revelation) the "Main Book," and not without reason; for the revelation of this Anointed One <term>Anointed One: translated from *Gesalbten*, the literal meaning of the title "Christ"</term> and sole Savior of the world satisfies every desire. It brings wisdom and knowledge with it, and through the radiance of His light, it opens all mysteries to be found in heaven and on earth (since all treasures are hidden in Him).

But because such precious little books might seem somewhat obscure <note>original: *dünckel*; meaning dark or difficult to understand.</note> to the unpracticed reader, due to their unfamiliarity and the lapsed memory of true knowledge <gloss>the</gloss>; therefore, the aforementioned **M. V. VV.** <note>Likely the initials of the editor or the person who compiled Lautensack's notes into this specific edition.</note> wished to apply the talent <note>original: *Pfundt*; literally "pound," referring to the Parable of the Talents where servants are expected to put their entrusted money to use.</note> entrusted to him by God for the common benefit of the godly. He intended, as far as the mysteries allow, to write them down with ink and compose them in human words,

Just as it is hoped that this will be pleasing and dear to the Christian reader that it has been brought into public print, so too, through the grace of God, it will not fail to be of great benefit, provided it is frequently read and contemplated with diligence.

<meta>This page continues the editorial preface, further explaining the additions made to Paulus Lautensack’s work to make it more accessible. [the editor has] explained the same with more figures and in a more detailed and familiar style, and pointed out, as if with a finger, how Christ alone is to be found in the center of the Cross. ->Preface<-

<meta>catchword: entire-</meta>

Although the Revelation of John is held in suspicion by many in these times of coarse ignorance, yet they can bring forth no other cause for this than their own lack of understanding; for which same reason the 4th Book of *Esdras* <note>original: *Esdrae*; a book of the Apocrypha often associated with apocalyptic visions</note> was also cast out from the number of Biblical books. But those who are the cause of this will find out in due time how just their judgment was. Since the Revelation—as the truth of Him who is truthful—is said to be hidden and incomprehensible, it is only hidden among the unbelievers; otherwise, it would not have been called the "Revelation of Jesus Christ" in vain, which was commanded to be shown only to His servants.

Although it is now objected by many that we have no need to ponder such hidden things, since the will of God is revealed as clear as day throughout the entire Scripture, and especially in the New Testament, yet they should also know (to say nothing of other reasons) that even though the will of God was clearly described in the Old Testament within the Law Books of Moses, and how every person should behave toward their Creator God and Lord, and yet in the most important matters, both private and public—that is, those which concern individual persons as well as an entire army, an entire lineage, a

He asserts that while the basic will of God is clear in the Law and the New Testament, prophetic insights remain necessary for specific personal and communal guidance.</summary>

### To the Reader.

entire community, entire land, or kingdom—affecting both future and present times—God has uniquely announced and proclaimed His unchangeable will through His dear friends, servants, and prophets. # Grace and true knowledge in God
### to the only one through the Revelation of Jesus
# CHRIST.

Honorable, Favorable Lady, since the world neither wants to know nor hear of the Revelation of the one Main Book <note>original: *Hauptbuchs Offenbarunge*; likely referring to the Book of Revelation or the Bible as the primary source of truth</note>, along with its mysteries contained therein, when such is shown to them, they shake their heads over it as if the Book did not concern them at all. Rather, they [claim to] already have a good knowledge beforehand of that which is to be known of God, from which it also becomes apparent what failings and lacks they still have regarding the Revelation of Jesus Christ. Therefore, He does not unjustly promise us another coming (according to the Spirit) to reassure our consciences before the Judgment <term>Gericht: the final divine assessment of souls</term> comes, so that we may be found blameless on the Last Day <term>jüngsten Tag: the Day of Judgment at the end of the world</term>.

Now, let the world take its understanding before it; likewise, I will also do so with the Image of Christ <note>original: *Bildtnus Christi*</note>. If we then reach an agreement where nothing more is noted, I will gladly stand aside with Christ, and my indication and painting shall be esteemed as nothing. (But may God forbid such a thing.) However, so that this may stand firmly and give testimony to the world—that it does not wish to recognize or accept its God and Lord in His own work, despite how abundantly He paints Himself before our eyes—as I have promised you, once I am finished with the work, I shall show this to you, as much as the Son of God is revealed at this time.

Therefore, my Lady, I wish first to draft for you the work of Christ with my fleshly understanding <note>The author refers to his "fleshly" or "carnal" understanding as his limited human perspective before divine enlightenment</note> (as much as God has granted me grace). Later, once you have the foundation and understand it, Christ will be more certain and more true to you than I and all men could testify to you, and

page-num 8.

The **New Testament** concludes with the two "times" <note>likely referring to two distinct eras or dispensations within the New Testament era</note>, within which are embodied **Matthew, Mark, Luke, John,** and the **Acts of the Apostles**; these then describe the one God: **Father, Son, and Holy Spirit**, and the **Word**.

The third foundation lies under the **Ox** <note>The ox is the traditional symbol for the Evangelist Luke</note> by the door, which the third cross with the "half-time" concludes; within this, the entire world is embodied, and the four **Evangelists** with their **twenty-four wings** <note>The four "living creatures" of Revelation 4:8 each have six wings; four creatures times six wings equals twenty-four</note> enclose God and the worthy humanity of Christ; this humanity concludes the **Book of Revelation**, and everything is incorporated into it.

Thus, upon the three pastures <note>original: "weiden"; this likely refers to the background fields or decorative panels in the author's visual diagram</note>, the three Persons—**Father, Son, and Holy Spirit**—are seen in imagery. There are also three figures: the first by the Father and the **Angel Matthew** <note>The angel or man is the symbol for the Evangelist Matthew</note>; the second [as a] **Sun** in the face of the Son of God; the third **Sun** wherein the image of **Mary** stands with her dear little child. These primarily conclude the three foundations within the three crosses and the three times. These "Persons" are also indicated by the **three comet stars** that appeared in years past, by which the three divine Persons are signified.

Conversely, each foundation and cross encloses the three divine Persons through the one Spirit and Word. Thus, in the three crosses, **nine suns** are seen, just as God has shown three suns appearing for the third time <note>A reference to "parhelia" or sun dogs, which were often interpreted as miraculous Trinitarian omens in the 17th century</note> and other signs in years past, thereby to reveal the foundation of the **Holy Spirit**.

So also on the first golden pillars, God is indicated six times, just as the one Spirit is depicted in the **Old Testament** in the **Second Book of Moses** <note>Exodus</note>, where He speaks in the third chapter, in the second section, which always indicates the Person of the Father. *When the Lord saw that he turned aside to see, God called to him out of the bush and said: Moses, Moses! He answered: Here am I, etc. and He spoke further: I am the GOD...*

<sig>B</sig>

...of your fathers, the God of Abraham and the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob; and Moses veils his face, for he was afraid to look upon God <note>This refers to Exodus 3:6, where God speaks to Moses from the burning bush</note>. Here, "God" is indicated six times, but the fifth [instance], the single Cross, concludes with the Old Testament; by Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, the three divine Persons are figured. And the sixth word "God"—the Person of the Son—concludes in the New Testament <note>The author is performing a "word count" analysis of the biblical text to find hidden patterns</note>. Thus, the foundation of the single Spirit is witnessed this time by the five books of Moses.

Since the foundation of the single Spirit is to be noted, as to what its final conclusion and content may be, the single Person of that same content is also embodied within it: namely God the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, and the Word. This then indicates the humanity of Christ, the Savior <term>saluator: Savior</term>. These are recorded upon the golden columns; and in the middle is the Person of the Son with the Greek Ω <note>original: "W"; the author likely refers to the Greek letter Omega (Ω), which resembles a rounded 'W' and signifies Christ as the "End" or "Conclusion"</note>. <meta>catchword: strong</meta>

...invisible in the Old Testament, but has now become visible as the true Messiah <note>original: "Messias"</note>, who was promised to the Jews. Just as they did not want to accept him, we Christians likewise want neither to know nor hear of the Book of Life <note>A biblical concept from Revelation and Psalms referring to the register of those who will inherit eternal life</note>, much less to accept it. This is because we do not care to recognize the external image of Christ which is set and painted before our eyes, much less to investigate the spirit within him; which is indeed impossible, otherwise God would not be God. 

However, since he has further granted us such grace that he gives himself and secures such mysteries within himself—through which we are comforted and strengthened in our faith to obtain a certain, joyful conscience—and yet we do not want it: woe to us! <meta>This page continues the explanation of the mystical diagrams or "Crosses" introduced on page 38. It details the method of reading these diagrams—both horizontally and vertically—to uncover the hidden unity of God within the scriptures of the Old and New Testaments.</meta>

<page-num>39.</page-num>
<meta>catchword: Of:</meta>

...what they signify and conclude will be seen in these present Crosses <term>Creußen: the symbolic diagrams or "visualizations" used by the author to map spiritual truths</term>, primarily the greatest and highest mystery <note>original: "geheymnus"</note> that is in the Scripture: namely, how at last a single God is to be recognized. In the Holy Scripture, there are two ways to find this, by means of the "crucifixion" of the Scripture <note>likely referring to the act of arranging or interpreting the text according to the vertical and horizontal axes of a cross</note>: namely, by moving forward <note>horizontally</note> and, secondly, by descending downward <note>vertically</note>. All of this is now to be seen before one's eyes and is manifest; if it is only shown to someone so that they understand it, then Christ is already revealed to them through the entire Bible, setting aside all human understanding and wisdom. For Christ does not accept testimony from men, because he well knows how to speak of himself and make himself known.

First, one should carefully observe the path of descending downward, which every Cross possesses and to which it testifies, as in the first three chapters of Revelation <note>original: "offenbahrugen"</note>. Each of these chapters has its own Cross, in which it concludes by descending through its five distinctions, and thus remains hanging on the Cross by itself.

Thus the first Cross contains "God" five times within itself, and yet is indicated to be only one Spirit. Nevertheless, that same single Spirit depicts itself as "God" six times in the **Old Testament**. One might wonder: which could be the true God among them? For the world is prone to mock and scoff at such indications, and yet it remains standing in truth for itself, hanging upon the Cross.

Likewise, the middle Cross also has a special indication of persons incorporated into it, and yet only one person is indicated thereby—specifically the first word, (**God**), which then concludes the number six as the true, single God. This is seen and found in the path of descending downward, in the single Person who still hangs and remains on the Cross.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;If one then "sinks" <note>A mystical term for meditative integration or finding one concept within another</note> the entire Book of **Revelation**, with its order and conclusion as shown above, into the first and other similar books, then it encloses them all within itself. In this, seventy-seven Cherubim are woven upon ten tapestries <term>teppicht: the curtains or woven hangings of the biblical Tabernacle described in Exodus 26</term>, and every two tapestries are joined together with fifty **loops** <term>knäuflin: literally "little knobs" or loops used to join the Tabernacle curtains</term>. 

Thus, the First Book of Moses [Genesis] has fifty chapters, in which four hundred and fifty <note>original: "fünffthalbhundert," an archaic German numerical expression for four-and-a-half hundred</note> distinctions are incorporated; thus seven Cherubim still remain over, which are indicated in the Book of **Revelation**. So further another tapestry is indicated, with eleven tapestries, in which a great mystery is revealed. The first tapestry with its loops has enclosed the First Book of Moses; but the other with eleven tapestries is concluded by the Book of Revelation with the **seven angels**. Thus the eleventh tapestry also has fifty loops, which then indicate the distinctions in the first part of the Book of the Revelation of Christ. However, these distinctions are only forty-six, so that four are still missing for there to be fifty. Which four distinctions shall be restored or fulfilled, as indicated by the foundation of the Holy Spirit: namely, that from all peoples—Jews, Gentiles, Christians—God wills to save some, who shall restore these loops and distinctions.

From now on, we must look only to our Master Christ, to what He is indicating and revealing to us; we must remain and stand by that, for therein we are assured.

<margin>Note Well</margin>
But such revelation, your god and you say, is no longer necessary, because no further improvement springs from it, as everything we are duty-bound to know about Christ has already been richly revealed to us. This is indeed true; yet must Christ therefore be found a liar? He who promised us that another would come according to the Spirit before the judgment? For He says: *nothing shall remain hidden or concealed that shall not come to light* <note>referring to Matthew 10:26</note>, and everything written of Him must be fulfilled, down to a single point—in which work it is now seen and revealed for all to see.

<meta>This page continues the author’s direct address to his patron ("my lady"). 49.

<margin>G</margin>

Therefore, my lady, if you have a superior understanding of Christ, then present it openly. It will later be recognized which party's account is the most certain, based on the testimony of Holy Scripture; to that party, one should subsequently and rightly give belief. In this way, it will become revealed which party is truly the most learned in their God. Had you been learned in your God, you should have rightly punished me if I had shown you anything that was not the truth, rather than permitting me—like a fool who deals in fantasies—to paint such things. This is especially true since the cost came out of your own purse. Instead, you have acted against the truth and pressured me into following your wishes in several instances. As for the "foundation of Christ" <note>original: "grund Christi" — likely referring to a specific theological premise or a physical architectural foundation provided by the patron</note> you gave me, I hold it dear and worthy as a testimony that men do not wish to know or recognize Christ by accepting Him.

The second point is that I removed the stars from the crucifixes; in that, I acted clearly and against the truth. You also wished for me to remove the "five-fold God" <note>original: "fünffmal Gott" — possibly a reference to the name of God written five times or a symbolic representation of the five wounds of Christ associated with the deity</note> from the golden columns; had I done so, the entire work of the Holy Spirit would have been brought to nothing. Furthermore, the greatest and highest thing was earnestly commanded for the third time: that I should again remove the gilded Title of Christ <note>term: "Title of Christ" — likely the 'INRI' inscription or a more elaborate titulus above the altar or on the walls</note> on the four walls, which nonetheless encompasses everything that is displayed and painted in the entire cellar <note>original: "Seller" — likely referring to a vaulted lower chamber or a private devotional cellar-chapel</note>.

If you have now received such an understanding from your **GOD**—that such displays as I have mentioned should not be allowed to remain—then I may, along with Christ, freely conclude against him and say that he is a false teacher and an Antichrist <note>original: "Wieder Christ"</note>. That is to say, Christ convicts him, as is demonstrated and made manifest.

Therefore, my lady, do not take it ill that I speak out against you, since it concerns the honor and glory...

We all wish to know and be capable of much, especially we <term>Naturalists</term> <note>German: *Naturales* — likely referring to natural philosophers or those who study the physical world</note> [who] consider it praiseworthy to know, and consider it shameful to be ignorant or not know, as Cicero says. <note>original Latin: "Naturales Scire laudi ducimus, Ignorare & Nescire turpe ducimus"</note>

Now I wish to show you here the right way to recognize and know all things, in both the Heavenly and earthly light, and that without error: namely through the second part of <term>Know thyself</term> <note>original: *Gnothi seauton* (Greek: Γνῶθι σεαυτόν)</note>, etc. Know yourself, then you will recognize that from which you have come, and you will learn precisely that from which you were taken and created; that is, <term>all-things</term> <note>German: *alleding* — an archaic term for the totality of creation or the microcosm; the idea that man contains the entire universe within himself</note>, for you are all-things within yourself, no less than God; yes, you are a child of God, of his Kingdom. If you know yourself, then you have as much as God (— more accurately, knowledge makes man perfect). You are a <term>co-heir</term> <note>German: *mitterb* (Miterbe)</note> with Christ; but Christ is the true God, a King and Prince on Earth, a head of all peoples, heathens, and languages. If you know yourself, then you know Jesus Christ, God and Man; for just as He is, so are we also in this world (1 John 4). Whoever wishes to recognize and know all things in Heaven and Earth, and desires to come after this life into the Divine and <term>Angelic Wisdom</term> <note>German: *Englische Weißheit* — in this context, 'Englische' is the archaic German for 'Angelic' (from *Engel*), not the English language</note>, he must know God. The knowledge of God is the root of all wisdom and eternal life <note>original Latin: "Cognitio Dei est Radix omnis Sapientiæ & vitæ æternæ"</note> (John 17): "This is eternal life, that they may know you, the only true God, and Jesus Christ whom you have sent." <note>original Latin: "Hæc est vita æterna, vt cognoscant, te solum Deum verum, & quem misisti Iesum Christum"</note> <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> in the <term>Poimandres</term> <note>original: *Pimandro* — the first tractate of the *Corpus Hermeticum*</note> [says]: "This is the only salvation for man: the knowledge of God." <note>original Latin: "Hæc vnica salus homini, Cognitio Dei"</note> Now, however, Divine and Angelic Wisdom is eternal life itself: and eternal life itself is Heavenly and Angelic wisdom.

We all naturally desire to know and be capable of much, especially those of us who study nature. As **Cicero** says, *we consider it a matter of praise to know, and we consider it a disgrace to be ignorant or not to know* <note>original Latin: "Scire laudi ducimus, Ignorare & Nescire turpe ducimus"</note>.

Now I wish to show you here the right path to recognize and know all things, in both the heavenly and earthly light, and to do so without error. Namely, through the second part of **Know Thyself** <note>original Greek: "Gnothi seauton"</note>, etc. Know yourself, and in doing so you will recognize where you have come from, and you will learn about that very thing from which you were taken and created. That is, you will know all things, for you are all things within yourself, no less than God; indeed, you are a child of God, belonging to His kingdom. If you know yourself, you possess as much as God—only knowledge makes a person perfect. You are a co-heir with Christ; but Christ is the true God, a King and Prince on Earth, a Head of all peoples, heathens, and languages. If you know yourself, then you know **Jesus Christ**, God and man, for just as He is, so are we also in this world (1 John 4). Whoever desires to recognize and know all things in Heaven and Earth, and longs to come to Divine and <term>Angelic Wisdom</term> <note>The German text uses "Englische Weißheit," where "Englisch" refers to angels (*Engel*), not the English nation.</note> after this life, must know God. *The knowledge of God is the root of all wisdom and eternal life* <note>original Latin: "Cognitio Dei est Radix omnis Sapientiæ & vitæ æternæ"</note>, John 17: *This is eternal life: that they may know you, the only true God, and Jesus Christ, whom you have sent.* **Mercurius in the Pymander** <note>A reference to the *Poimandres*, a foundational text of Hermetic philosophy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus (Mercurius).</note>: *This is the only salvation for man: the knowledge of God.* Now, however, Divine and Angelic wisdom *is eternal life itself*: And *eternal life itself* is heavenly, Angelic wisdom.

Through this we become **born again** <note>original Latin: "Renati"</note>, Christians, angels, etc., [and] will be capable of all things like the angels. 

If we wish to learn to know God, however, we must not study it from the books of men, but from Him who teaches us everything we are capable of and know. The present world wants to hear nothing of this, which is why there are so few truly learned people in all lands; we desire only something worldly-wise and nothing Divine. If we—I say—are to recognize God rightly, we cannot see, seek, or find Him except in His seat and throne. No seat or throne is the **Infinity of the Abyss** <note>original Latin: "Infinitudo Abyssi"</note> where we naturally speculate about the eternal Godhead, as the **instructional primer** <note>original Latin: "informatorium"</note> teaches. We also must not wander around in the **circumference** <note>original Latin: "Circumferentia"</note>—that is, running back and forth within **creation** <note>original: "Creatur"</note>, though creation does lead to the Master Builder <note>The initials "R. I." likely refer to a specific cited author or text common in alchemical circles.</note>—but instead we must go out from both of these **to the center** <note>original Latin: "ad centrum"</note>, to the midpoint.

M.** <note>an abbreviation for "Iesus Christus Salvator Mundi," Latin for Jesus Christ, Savior of the World</note>, into the humanity of Christ; which one Person died on the cross, was taken down, laid in a grave, and rose from the dead on the third day. Not that this Person alone was crucified, died, and buried without the Spirit and the Word; no indeed, for otherwise God would not have died, the whole of God would not have become man, and the whole of GOD would not have died, been crucified, and buried for us, as the sects speak of it. 

For if we wish to recognize the whole God, we must have three Persons, and especially that God who says: "I am the first and the last, the **Alpha** <note>original Greek: "α"</note> and the **Omega** <note>original Greek: "ω"</note>, the Beginning and the End." That is, we must take the Alpha and the Omega together and unite them in essence: Heaven and Earth, eternity and time. Otherwise we would have only a half-knowledge, and would not even desire to have a whole knowledge, as all the **lettered men** <note>original Latin: "Literati," referring to those with academic but perhaps not spiritual learning</note> do, who know and understand nothing else. Whoever does not study these three books can never know himself, nor can he become a truly learned man, even if he knew ten, twelve, or fifteen languages and were the **Light of the world** <note>original: *Lux mundi*</note> in **Theology, Medicine, and Law** <note>original: *Theologia, Medicina & Iurisprudentia*</note>, as such **Doctors** <note>original: *Doctores*</note> are made in the high schools <note>Universities</note>.

Now we wish to look at our members <note>limbs or body parts</note> internally and externally, our entire body with all its joints; for we are Christ’s members; we are like God. Just as Adam became like one of us, so God has become like us; just as He is, so are we also in this world. Behold the middle cross, where God and Man hang, the Beginning and the End, the First and the Last; the first according to the Spirit and the Father, the last according to the Word or Holy Spirit. Thus you find 15 members <note>The author is likely referring to the segments or phalanges of the fingers/toes</note> on the hands and feet, along with the three nerves, according to the three crosses, which are all drawn into one single cross; for there is only one God, only one Christ. Now the cross has five points: first, four corners according to the four points of the Sun, the Moon, and the Stars; for in 24 hours the Sun goes around the Earth, as does the Moon, and likewise the morning and evening stars. <meta>This page continues the mystical anatomical analysis from page 9, detailing how the 36 letters of the alphabet correspond to the three crosses and the human body, specifically focusing on the hands, feet, and wounds of Christ as a map for understanding the Book of Revelation.</meta>

<page-num>10.</page-num>

...clothes the five points of the first **Cross** <note>The author is likely referring to a diagram where points on a cross are mapped to body parts</note>. To this, **Paulus Lautensack** <note>Paulus Lautensack (1478–1558) was a Nuremberg painter and organist who created a complex system of mystical diagrams connecting the human body to the Bible</note> takes 36 letters of the three languages with special words which he calls **Spirits** <note>In this context, "Spirits" refers to active divine forces or energies rather than "dead" written characters</note>. These are not dead letters, but the actions and powers of the three divine Persons. For the **Trinity** is revealed, together with all creatures, through the three crosses. Specifically, so that everyone may see with their own eyes and read the Scripture accordingly, he hangs the members of **Christ** upon the three crosses:

*a, b, c, d, e* are the five points of the first Cross; *f, g, h, i, k* are the five points of the middle Cross, representing the five internal wounds of **Christ**; *l, m, n, o, p* are the five fingers on the left hand on the third Cross. Together, these make 15 actions or powers, their 15 paragraphs from the first three chapters of the **Apocalypse** <note>The Book of Revelation</note> attached to them. These are later taken down and sunk into the other chapters, as if into their grave for the resurrection.

Furthermore, he clothes the third Cross a second time with the paragraphs of the 12th, 13th, and 14th chapters—for one only needs to study six chapters with their paragraphs to have studied all of Scripture. He uses 15 letters again, according to the 15 limbs of the external person and the 15 paragraphs of the 12th, 13th, and 14th chapters:

*q, r, s, t, u* clothe the first Cross—these are the five toes on the right foot; *x, y, z, &, z, ʒ* clothe the middle Cross, representing the five external wounds. For **Christ** is twofold, internal and external, just as all other humans are. *9, r, u, s* <note>These characters likely represent specific symbols in Lautensack’s system</note> clothe the third Cross, representing the five toes on the left foot. Thus, there are 30 small members—hands, external wounds, internal and external—which are spirits, actions, and powers. Through these, the whole of Scripture and the entire work of Scripture is to be studied, so that one may know **Christ** and understand oneself. Upon this foundation, not a letter, nor a book, nor a spirit shall be moved, any more than the limbs of the human body can be moved. 

So certain is this **study of knowing God, oneself, and all things in heaven and on earth** <note>original: *studium cognoscendi Deum, sei pSum, & omnia in coelo & in terra*</note>.

<page-num>11.</page-num>

When a person knows themselves, they have the true book within; from it, they learn all things. Oh, how slowly and with great toil and labor we learn from the books of men! The **Bible** is enough for all earthly and heavenly wisdom. But even the great "Articulators" <note>likely referring to the points of connection in Lautensack’s diagrams between the body and the cross</note> are crucified by these same letters and **Spirits** and hung upon the three crosses. For instance: **a, b, c** are the first points of the three crosses, representing the right arm with its three joints: **Spirit**, **Person**, **Word**. **b, g, m** are the other three points of the three crosses, signifying the left arm of **Christ** with the **Son** and **Father**. **c, h, n** are the lowest points of the three crosses, representing the left thigh with its three joints. **d, i, o** contain the four points of the three crosses and the right thigh with its three joints. **v, z**, representing **Humanity**, occupy the three center points of the three crosses and contain the head, chest, and navel. Here we see once again: *a time, two times, and half a time* <note>A reference to the prophetic time periods found in the Book of Daniel and Revelation, interpreted here as internal mystical states</note>; this is internal, according to **Jesus**—that is, according to the **Spirit**. Furthermore, externally according to the **Word**, these members are crucified once more: **q, r, g** the last arm <note>The German "lestt arm" likely refers to the final arm in the diagram's sequence</note> with three joints; **e, p, y** the left thigh with three joints; **t, x, r** the right thigh; **v, z** the head, chest, and navel.

Thus the whole **Christ**, internally and externally—that is, according to His height and width—is fastened to the **Cross**. Along with Him, all our own limbs witness that we bear **God's image** and are just as He is. We must be crucified, die, and be buried with Him, as the **Apostle** says in **1 Timothy chapter six** <note>The author likely refers to the general Pauline theology of dying with Christ, usually found in Romans 6, but cites 1 Timothy 6 regarding the "good confession"</note>, if we wish to rise with Him. This means: if we wish to rightly study, learn, and recognize **Holy Scripture** and all things. For whoever learns and recognizes Holy Scripture recognizes himself. <note>original: "Et qui seipsum cognoscit, etiam cognoscit Christum, & scripturas, & omnia"</note> **"And he who knows himself, also knows Christ, the scriptures, and all things."** Such a person carries all books within them, can walk in **God** at all hours, and arrives at all earthly and heavenly

There is nothing new here, but rather that which is *most ancient* <note>original Latin: "Antiquissimum"</note>, and therefore *most true* <note>original Latin: "Verissimum"</note>. Just as it is not a new thing to have five fingers on the hand, two legs, or two eyes in the head, so too is this study—*know yourself and all things* <note>original Latin: "Cognosce teipsum & omnia"</note>—not new. No scholar should excuse himself and say it is a strange or unheard-of thing to crucify the Scripture through letters or spirits and to assign a limb to every spirit; it has been ordained so *from eternity* <note>original Latin: "ab æterno"</note>, just as our God himself was crucified. ## Being is by necessity twofold.

Being <note>The word used is *Wesen*, which can mean essence, being, or substance.</note> that is uncreated, or not brought forth, or depending on nothing: 2. Being that is created or brought forth, or depends on something.
II. For uncreated Being is of itself, in itself, through itself, requires nothing, and is therefore Unity <note>original: "Unitet," from the Latin *unitas*</note>, which is sufficient unto itself: Created being is not of itself, nor in itself, nor through itself, but is from another, in another, and through another, and is therefore duality <note>original: "zweyheit"</note>; for the second number depends upon Unity.
III. Again, the uncreated being is by necessity simple <note>meaning "single" or "without parts"</note>, just like Unity, but the created being is by necessity twofold, or double, as the duality or the falling away from Unity: For the being of the creature is derivative <note>original: *herkomblich*</note> or foreign, because what it is and has, that same being it is and has from God, who is uncreated; therefore, that which belongs to the creature is nothing, or non-being; for of itself, it is nothing, and would be nothing, before it was made.
IV. Every being is either satisfied with itself, or desires to go toward that from which it has its origin; Or, all things desire to return to where they came from: God is satisfied with Himself, and is sufficient unto Himself, and contains Himself, and is in

15.
B iij

# On the Fall.

### That the creature, when it bends back or turns into itself, is deceived or deceived by itself; that is, that the creature, when it inclines or winds toward Being <note>original: "Wesen" - can also mean essence or substance</note>, is deceived by itself.

## Being is necessarily of two kinds.

I. Being that is uncreated, or not brought forth, or dependent on nothing: 2. being that is created or brought forth, or dependent on something.
II. For uncreated Being is of itself, in itself, and through itself, requires nothing, and is therefore the Unity <note>original: "Vnitet," from the Latin "unitas"</note>, which is sufficient unto itself: Created being is not of itself, nor in itself, nor through itself, but is from another, in another, and through another, and is therefore duality <note>original: "zweyheit"</note>; for the second number <note>meaning everything after the "One"</note> depends upon the Unity.
III. Furthermore, uncreated being is necessarily simple, just like the Unity; but created being is necessarily twofold or double, as the duality or the fall from Unity: For the being of the creature is derived or foreign; for whatever it is and has, that same being it has from God, who is uncreated. Therefore, the creature's own property is nothing, or "to be nothing"; for of itself, it is nothing, and would be nothing before it was made.
IV. Every being is either satisfied with itself, or it desires that from which it has its origin; or rather, all things desire that place from which they have come: God is satisfied with Himself, and is sufficient unto Himself, and contains Himself, and is in Him-

<summary>This page outlines a dualistic metaphysics, distinguishing between "Uncreated Being" (God/Unity) and "Created Being" (the creature/Duality). It argues that the creature possesses nothing of its own and falls into deception when it turns away from its source in God to focus on its own "nothingness."</summary>
<keywords>The Fall, Uncreated Being, Created Being, Unity, Duality, Essence, Metaphysics, God</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: This page follows the discussion of the metaphysical distinction between God as Unity and the creature as Duality. It explores why the creature’s inherent nature—being made from nothing—leads to a restless desire for "being" that allows the "recoiling snake" (the urge for self-sufficiency) to deceive it.</meta>

sufficient unto Himself / seeks no external support / and therefore He desires to go where He is from / but He is from no one / therefore He desires no one / but Himself: Likewise the creature <term>Creatur<gloss>any created thing, particularly human beings</gloss></term> desires to go where it is from / or desires to return there / from where it has come / Now it is not without a beginning / or eternal like God / but it is made out of nothing into something / or out of nothing into a being <note>original: "wesen" – can mean essence, substance, or an existing entity</note> / therefore it desires after being / and cannot be satisfied with itself / for duality <note>original: "zweyheit"</note> is a fall from Unity <note>original: "Vnitet"</note>. Therefore when it turns back to itself it finds two / namely the foreign, derived being / which it has received from God / and its own nothingness / which is its own property.

*V.* The creature recognizes of itself / that it is not of itself / but from another / and that it hangs upon another / namely upon the One / therefore it seeks after itself / and also after the One or after God / and thus, out of the desire to be / it is driven around in circles / therefore the desire to be is the recoiling snake <note>original: "widerbiegende Schlang" – a reference to the serpent in Eden, here personified as the creature's own self-centered desire</note>.

*VI.* The creature also recognizes within itself / that it is bound to obedience / that is, to the preservation of the nature and property of the Image <note>referring to the "Imago Dei," the image of God in which humans were created</note> / or it sees out of itself / that it should preserve the character and property of a formation <note>original: "Bildunge"</note> / yet it is deceived by the crooked / cunning / recoiling snake / so that it eats from the tree of the knowledge of good and evil. The cause: the creature desires being / desires to be / and to be good / and to be like God / or to be its own self just like God. There is nothing more lovely than Being and self-existence / therefore "to be" is proper to GOD alone / since He alone is the true Being / but it is proper to the creature to be nothing / and yet it is / that is, it has a contingent being <note>original: "zufelliges sein" – in theological philosophy, this refers to an "accidental" or "non-necessary" existence that depends entirely on God’s will</note>.

<summary>This page explains that because creatures are made from nothing, they possess an inherent "nothingness" and a restless desire to achieve true "being." This desire for self-sufficiency is identified as the "snake" that leads to the Fall, as the creature tries to be its own master like God.</summary>
<keywords>The Fall, Unity vs Duality, Being and Nothingness, the Snake, Contingent Being, Image of God, Self-existence</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page concludes the discussion of the creature's fall from Unity into Duality. It describes how the creature (the soul) attempts to claim "being" for itself, which leads to spiritual death and the need for Christ to crush the self-centered will.</meta>

Therefore, when it <gloss>the creature or soul</gloss> comes into itself / and ascribes being to itself / and desires to exist / it then becomes a thief / because it attributes to itself a foreign God / and a murderer / because it kills itself / and now it becomes aware of its own nothingness / out of which it was made / it wishes to seize the *Being of Things* <note>original: "Rerum Esse" – the ultimate reality or essence of all that exists</note> / the essence of all things and the One / but instead it seizes *Accidental Being* <note>original: "Esse accidentale" – a philosophical term for temporary or non-essential qualities that do not constitute true substance</note> / the incidental essence / which cannot be sufficient for itself: Thus it is trapped and deceived by itself / whereas it should have preserved the kind / nature and quality of the Image <note>referring to the "Imago Dei" or the Image of God in which the creature was created</note>.

   If the recoiling will <note>original: "widerbiegende will" – the self-centered desire that turns away from God and back toward the self, previously compared to a snake</note> is now the snake / *therefore* <note>original: "Ergo"</note> Christ must crush its head / through hellish anguish / so that the [self-centered] will or the [corrupt] nature may be killed.

# A BRIEF PATH,
## OR PERFECT METHOD
### to True Theology, that is, to every
### kind of
### science.
<note>original title: "COMPENDIOSA VIA, SEV PERFECTA METHODVS ad veram Theologiam, h. e. ad omne Genus scientiarum."</note>

To philosophize not in a pagan, but in a Christian manner—that is, to correctly learn the entire foundation of truth without error—along the narrow, small path in both lights, that of Nature and that of Grace <note>The "two lights" is a concept often found in Paracelsian and mystical thought: the Light of Nature (reason and observation) and the Light of Grace (divine revelation).</note>, it is necessary to know the fountainhead and origin of all wisdom in the Heavenly and earthly light, which is **True Theology** <note>original: *Vera Theologia*</note>. This remains for all Christians the beginning, middle, and end. From it, one learns not only the way to eternal life, but all other knowledge and sciences flow from her, and must be learned and taken from her; she remains at all times the **Mistress and Lady of all Sciences and Faculties** <note>original: *Magistra & Domina omnium Scientiarum atque Facultatum*</note>. 

Whoever thoroughly knows and understands **Theology** is the true **Philosopher, Physician, Doctor, Astronomer, Legal Expert, Mage, Alchemist, etc.** <note>original: *Philosophus, Physicus, Medicus, Astronomus, Iurisperitus, Magus, Alchymista*</note> She is the foundation and ground of all Heavenly and earthly wisdom, which the high schools <note>referring to universities</note> together with all worldly scholars have until now neither known nor believed: she is the single book from which all books must be made, written, read, and understood.

<summary>This page introduces 'True Theology' as the singular, divine source of all earthly and heavenly knowledge, positioning it as superior to secular university education and the essential foundation for all sciences and arts.</summary>
<keywords>Christian philosophy, True Theology, Light of Nature, Light of Grace, foundation of science, alchemy, medicine, astronomy, higher education</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the geometric and astronomical allegory of the cross, transitioning from the description of the New Jerusalem as a square to the mapping of Christ’s crucifixion onto the celestial bodies and the structure of the manuscript itself.</meta>

29.
D iij

The line A, C, drawn from the Sun through the **center of the earth** <note>original Latin: "Centrum terræ"</note> into the **lunar body** <note>original Latin: "Corpus Lunare"</note> provides the **upright beam of the cross** <note>original Latin: "Lignum crucis"</note> reaching upward; but the line B, D, drawn from the Evening Star into the Morning Star, creates the **crossbeam**; through this we see that the sole Godhead, or Spirit, is entitled to the Word—which Word is God—where the day merges into the night and the night into the day. Between these two is the Evening Star, situated between day and night; although it is only one star, it is nonetheless also the Morning Star, for it is internal and external according to its Person and humanity. He is God and man; He is the Day and the Light, and the Morning Star, through which God and the Word reveal themselves according to the Heavenly and earthly kingdoms. In His crucifixion, two additional crosses are seen on either side, through which the entire **Trinity** <note>original: "Trinitet"</note> is revealed: the cross on the right hand is assigned to the Spirit or Father, which is why the thief on the right hand went with Christ into Paradise, as he performed penance for his sins.

The cross on the left hand signifies the Word, the Holy Spirit, and the entire world; this thief would accept no grace, just as the world even to this day is accustomed to contradicting the Holy Spirit and is condemned.

All things are hung with Christ upon the cross, and all things are recognized in the center-point of the middle cross, as in the Person. Therefore, just as Scripture must be hung with Christ on the three crosses, taken down, and laid in a new grave, the Author indicates to us that there are no more than six chapters to be studied, with their thirty paragraphs or distinctions, where each chapter or distinction has its assigned letter or spirit according to the Latin alphabet. For such spirits, words, or workings, along with their letters, govern the entire work, so that one

But the reason why every color is seen threefold is, first, that the first three points come out of the yellow field of the three crosses and are immersed with their spirits into the first three chapters a. b. c., just as the first three paragraphs <note>distinctions or specific divisions within the text</note> each require and occupy a special chapter for themselves.

Furthermore, in the blue field there are again three paragraphs, namely B. G. M., taken from the blue point of the three crosses, and immersed into their 3 chapters, as if into their grave, **"so that they may declare and be declared"** <note>original Latin: *vt declarent & declarentur*</note>.

Likewise, the green field—that is, the three distinctions d. i. o.—are immersed into the chapters k. l. m. together with the Fathers and books, **"so that they may declare and be declared."**

But the reason why on every page 3 paragraphs are immersed into their 3 chapters is that Spirit, Person, and Word must always be together; for the Word does nothing without the Spirit, and the Spirit nothing without the Word, and these two do nothing at all without the Person.

What is Person, but Spirit and Word? And what is Spirit and Word, but Person? These three must be together and inseparable, just as Father, Son, and Holy Spirit are distinct <note>yet unified</note>.

Those who wish to recognize the heavenly eternal Persons—that is, according to the Spirit—or the **Trinity** <term>Trinity: the Christian doctrine of one God in three persons (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit)</term> only according to the three physical, temporal Persons—that is, according to the Word—instead believe that the knowledge of the **Trinity** is found in what is said in the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*; the primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, 1530</note>: that the Father is in heaven, and the Son is on earth, and the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son. They do not unite [these views] etc. and <unclear>w.</unclear> together so that they might have a perfect understanding **of the Trinity** <note>original: *Trinitatis*</note> **and of Theology** <note>original: *Theologiæ*</note>. Likewise, the Word is not without Spirit and Person (represented as &c.).

When the Scripture speaks of the Spirit, then Person and Word are included at the same time; and when the Scripture speaks of the Person, then the Word and the Spirit are included at the same time; and when the Scripture speaks of the Word, then the Person and Spirit are also included at the same time. Therefore, the four colors of the half-rainbow stand ninefold through the first twelve chapters, just as in the other half of this book the four colors also stand ninefold.

<meta>This page continues the author's complex structural mapping of theological concepts. ## In this great work, it should be noted:

1. Upon the Crosses.
2. Upon two half-rainbows.

9. Upon the three ranks <note>original: *Reygen* (rows or choral ranks)</note> of the words and spirits.

In summary, however one may create paintings and figures, there remain no more than two images and three crosses, three times or three persons—heavenly and earthly—and four colors in the rainbow. One neither may nor can step out of or beyond these, just as no single creature can be outside the city <note>original: *extra Ciuitatem*</note> of God, or just as the sun cannot go beyond Cancer <note>original: *ultra Cancrum*</note> or Capricorn <note>original: *vel Capricornum*</note>; it must remain in the ecliptic line <note>original: *linea Ecliptica*</note> and depart from us out of Cancer, and return to us again out of Capricorn. Likewise, one can bring forth nothing new or foreign in this; it must remain with the two images, God and the Word, and with the aforementioned points concerning the sun, stars, moon, etc. Nothing else will come of it.

This study is so certain that whoever learns it from Lautensack <note>Paulus Lautensack (1491–1558), a German organist and mystic artist known for his complex theological diagrams mapping the human body to the divine.</note> according to his instruction learns nothing other than himself, and that from which he was made and taken. I say nothing foreign, nothing new—as Paulus Lautensack presents it—other than what we ourselves are, and that from which we were taken, and what we have and carry within us and on our limbs and joints, internally and externally. # CONCERNING THE MIRACULOUS WORK,
<note>original: "DE OPERE MIRABILI"</note>
## Of the wondrous work of the Holy Spirit,
### how it should be established and performed.

It is called the **Miraculous Work** <note>original: "Opus Mirabile"</note> for this reason: because all books, Church Fathers, and creatures in heaven and earth are seen in this table, how they all lie with **Christ** on the **Cross**, and that the single morning star encloses and encompasses the seven and twelve stars—indeed, the eleven [stars], sun, and moon—within itself. <note>These celestial numbers refer to biblical visions, specifically Joseph’s dream in Genesis and the woman clothed with the sun in the Book of Revelation.</note> That is to say: all spirits, words, books, Fathers, and creatures in time and in eternity; heaven, hell, angels, devils, the blessed, and the damned—nothing is excluded.

Secondly, it is also called the **Miraculous Work** because in the single center point of the **Cross** there is a beginning, middle, and end of the **Trinity** <note>original: "Trinitet"</note> and a throne or seat, wherein three eternal and three temporal persons reveal themselves together with all that they have made and created in heaven and earth. All things have flowed out from Him and must return back in. Whoever knows Him, for that person the seven seals on the closed book are broken, and he possesses the understanding of all writings and books in the entire world. It is the Book of Life and includes all books—that is, all people, nations, Jews, heathens, Christians, etc. It is the **Revelation**, <note>original: "Apocalypsis"</note> the

<sig>H iij</sig>

It claims that understanding this central mystery grants the viewer insight into all worldly and spiritual knowledge, effectively acting as the "Book of Life."</summary>

Main Book and encompasses all books of the Old and New Testaments. And just as Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypsis"</note> is a written key to open up all writings, so too is the Lamb—the Person—the key to opening all things that lie hidden in books, in creatures, and in humans. Without the Main Book of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypsis"</note>, no one is a true theologian <note>original: "Theologus"</note>; without Christ, no one can possess heavenly or earthly wisdom. He is the sun and encompasses all eleven suns—that is, all spirits. <note>This likely refers to the biblical vision of Joseph, where eleven stars/suns bow to the twelfth, here identified as Christ.</note> He is the moon—that is, the Word—and encompasses all words, such as Majesty, GOD the Father, GOD the Son, God the Holy Spirit, Word, etc. He is the morning and evening star—that is, the Person—and encompasses all persons, etc. He is the day and night, month, week, feast, year, age, and all times.

Thirdly, it is also called the **Miraculous Work** <note>original: "Opus Mirabile"</note> due to its supreme certainty <note>original: "propter summam certitudinem"</note>, for it is not a newly invented thing by humans, but rather ordained and predestined <note>original: "prædestiniert"</note> by the Holy Spirit from eternity. This is so that in the four fields—according to the four colors of the rainbow <note>original: "Iridis"</note>—every book and every Church Father, with their letters and numbers, remains within their designated place, space <note>original: "Spacio"</note>, and order. For if even one letter or word were displaced or transposed in any of the three main languages <note>The "three languages" refer to the sacred tongues of the Cross: Hebrew, Greek, and Latin.</note>, the entire work would be false. This most divine study <note>original: "diuinissimum Studium"</note> is so certain that it should entice and lure all people to seek and study the single morning star in the center point of the Cross. Whoever does so remains written in the **Book of Life**, and possesses the perfect knowledge of all heavenly and earthly wisdom.

All things are ordained and enclosed within the single Book of the Lamb, so certainly that every letter of the alphabet in the three languages <note>original: "Litera Alphabeti trium Linguarum"</note>, along with the books and the Church Fathers, must keep its own compartment, space <note>original: "Spatium"</note>, and order, whether the Fathers and books are found broken or unbroken.

<meta>This page continues the definition of the "Miraculous Work" (Opus Mirabile), transitioning from its celestial and scriptural origins to its internal presence within the human being. It emphasizes that all true knowledge is found through self-reflection.</meta>

Fourthly, it might also be called the **Miraculous Work** <note>original: "Opus Mirabile"</note> because it leads a person without error to that which they already possess within themselves. It shows that throughout our lives, we can learn or study nothing else but what is already in us, and that from which we were taken and made.

We are made from the **clay of the earth** <note>original: "Limo terræ"; a reference to the biblical creation of man in Genesis</note>, and it is within us; thus we study the **clay of the earth**. We are from God, from Christ; therefore we learn nothing but Christ and God. We are from the Scripture, and it is entirely within us; therefore we learn nothing but what is in us. Here lies the true **Know thyself** <note>original: "Γνῶθι σεαυτὸν"</note>. Know yourself, and so you shall know Christ and all things in heaven, on earth, and in hell.

When this **divine study** <note>original: "diuinum Studium"</note> is opened to the world, no one will be able to lead another astray. All false books and people will be cast away and burned. Everyone will see with their own eyes and not **beyond the eye** <note>original: "ulterius oculo"; likely meaning one will no longer need to rely on the perceptions or interpretations of others</note>, for a person will read their own book, of which they previously knew nothing.

A person is that which they study and learn. Christ is the **Miraculous Work**, and the **Miraculous Work** is Christ. And more: man is the **Miraculous Work**, and the **Miraculous Work** is man. He carries it in his hands and feet, in his internal and external limbs; and he remains a book written within and without, just like Christ—so that just as He is, so are we also in this world.

--- Page 168 ---
<meta>This page follows a fragmentary discussion of the "internal heaven." Here, the author shifts to the "internal hell" and expands into a critique of academic learning versus the mystical, self-derived knowledge exemplified by the work of Paulus Lautensack.</meta>

65.

To come into hell and become a child of hell, hell must first be within a person. Nevermore could a person come into hell if hell were not already in them. If a person is to learn the Scripture, then the Scripture must be in them; for we learn that of which we are made, and that which is within us.

Oh, you worldly-learned ones, how have you for such a long time misled and darkened yourselves with false books and **professions** <note>original: "Professionibus"; referring to formal academic offices or public declarations of faith</note>, and held back and failed to recognize the Light that is in you all. Know yourselves, and thus you know all things.

Now, to perform the **Marvelous Work** <note>original: "**Opus Mirabile**"; likely a reference to the magnum opus of alchemy or a specific mystical treatise</note>, know that **Paulus Lautensack** <note>A 16th-century Nuremberg organist and visionary artist known for his complex theological diagrams</note> made exactly that which was in him, which he himself was, both internally and externally. And I, who <note>NB</note> <note>"NB" stands for "Nota Bene," meaning "Note Well."</note> through God’s grace have been taught by instruction from him, make exactly that which is in me, which I have become through study. And when I learn to make such things and set them down, it comes entirely into agreement with the Author; not a section, **space** <note>original: "Spacium"</note>, nor letter is displaced. Thus, reader, it will be for you as well, when you learn it completely: that you write out of yourself what is in you—what you have recognized without the books of men. And you will marvel that so many hundreds of **writers** <note>original: "**Scribenten**"</note> in **theology** <note>original: "**Theologia**"</note> and all other faculties have maintained no foundation; for they have borrowed and taken it out of paper and human books. They have traveled around in their studying and writing in the **circumference** <note>original: "**Circumferentia**"; the outer edges or superficial aspects of a subject</note>, and have not paid attention to the **center** <note>original: "**Centrum**"</note>, to the **Number Five** <note>original: "**Numerum Quinarium**"; in mystical thought, this often refers to the "Quinary" or the microcosm of man (five senses, four elements plus the soul)</note>—which is to say, to themselves—as the **Marvelous Work** indicates and requires. Such interpreters of Scripture should have looked upon the **Mercy Seat** <note>original: "**Gnadenstuhl**"; the golden cover of the Ark of the Covenant where God's presence rested</note>, so that not out of the first nor the other **cherub** <note>original: "**Cherubin**"</note>, but in the middle between the two wings, the bodily voice might be heard. That is: the right and true answer proceeds not out of the Spirit alone, nor out of the Word alone, but out of the Person into whom God and the Word have immersed themselves.
