<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for constructing a mystical grid. The previous page established the mapping of letters and the genealogy of Jesus (the 77 Fathers) into specific spaces. This page focuses on the concept of "breaking" or "opening" the sequences through mathematical permutations involving the numbers 7 and 11, drawing parallels to the crucifixion of Christ and the seals of Revelation.</meta>

77. Letters with the 77 Fathers and Books, and all point to the right Book and Father in the **center point** <note>original: *Mittelpunct*</note>. You must also observe and keep this regarding the ***M.*** and ***a.*** and the four letters ***R S W N*** in the other three fields; and all letters must remain unmoved alongside their Fathers and Books, otherwise the whole work would be wrong. Likewise, the numbers remain unmoved—namely, when Fathers and Books remain unbroken, as in the word **Jesus** and **King** <note>original: *Rex*</note>.

But when the Fathers and Books are **broken** <note>meaning rearranged or decoded through a specific cipher</note> by the eleven suns or moons or eleven stars—as in the word **Nazarene** <note>original: *Nazarenus*</note> and **of the Jews** <note>original: *Iudæorum*</note>—then they remain with the letters and **spaces** <note>original: *Spatien*; the cells of the grid</note> in their previous order, except that the numbers are transposed. This shows that the Fathers and Books belong in their suns and moons as "broken" Fathers and Books.

Therefore one says: 11 times seven Fathers—there they are broken. But when one says: seven times 11 Fathers, then they are unbroken, and the numbers stay in their order. Likewise, when one speaks of eleven times seven Books, there the seals are unbroken, and the numbers stay in their order. But when one speaks of eleven times 7 Books, then they are broken.

For if the Scripture were not **crucified** <note>a mystical term for being broken apart and rearranged to reveal hidden meaning</note> nor broken, one could not come to a true understanding. Therefore, just as the **Bible** <note>original: *Biblia*</note> remains sealed and unbroken through the seven stars and lampstands, so through the eleven suns, moons, or eleven stars, it is opened through the **breaking** <note>original: *Brechung*</note>. The book lies closed with seven seals, as the seven stars and lampstands indicate; but through the eleven suns and eleven moons, the seven seals are opened through the breaking.

Further, begin to write the Fathers into their spaces at the word **Nazarene** <note>original: *Nazarenus*</note> according to their order and according to the three alphabets; then everything follows as before, except that you change the numbers.

<meta>This page continues the technical and mystical explanation of the decoding system. Having previously established the visual use of "crosses" to represent the analysis of texts, the author now explains the three components necessary for a complete understanding of the Book of Revelation and Holy Scripture, framing the process as a spiritual death and resurrection of the Word.</meta>

73.

Even though this foundational work, the **crucifixion of the paragraphs** <note>original: *Creutzigung der Paragraphorum*; here referring to the mystical method of dissecting the text to reveal hidden meanings</note>, is distinct from the breaking of the **seven seals** in the **Miraculous Work** <note>original: *Opere Mirabili*</note> through the eleven suns and moons, etc.—nonetheless, one cannot exist without the other. One must possess, understand, and master both together; otherwise, the **Great Work of the Holy Spirit** could not be achieved.

One must have three things if one wishes to study the **Book of Revelation** <note>original: *Apocalypsin*</note> along with the **Holy Scripture** thoroughly.

The first is the **Miraculous Work** <note>original: *Opus Mirabile*</note>, in which the seven seals are broken by the eleven suns, moons, etc.

The second part consists of the **three crosses**, upon which the **paragraphs** of the **six chapters** are crucified by their spirits, broken, taken down, and lowered into their respective chapters as if into a grave for their resurrection.

The third part is the **Great or Universal Work of the Holy Spirit** <note>original: *Opus Magnum vel Vniuersum Spiritus sancti*</note>, which must be fashioned out of these first two. For without these two, it is impossible to perform such a **Great Work**; and without these three, it is impossible to study the **Book of Revelation**—that is, the whole of Scripture and all things—through the provided means. Unless, of course, one wished to study Scripture without means—that is, without the **thirty-six spirits**—through the Holy Spirit directly, as the prophets, **apostles**, and all the **reborn** <term>Renati: those who have undergone a spiritual rebirth or transformation</term> have done and still do.

One might ask: "If one can study and learn the **Book of Revelation** and all Scripture through the Holy Spirit, why then are these means and such instructions necessary?" Answer: Although one uses such means, one still cannot come to any understanding of Scripture without the Holy Spirit. One must likewise be taught by God. For this reason, one crucifies the Scripture, takes it down, and lays it in a **new grave**, so that Christ, together with the whole of Scripture, may rise from the dead.

74.

Such means are not given so that a person could learn the **Revelation** <term>Apocalypsin: The Book of Revelation, here standing for the entirety of divine mystery</term>—that is, the whole of Scripture—on their own; if a person were capable of that, then God would not be God. Rather, these means are provided so that through them, one may practice and prepare oneself to become a student of the **Holy Spirit**, and so that we humans may have a double witness—inward and outward, of the spirit and of the letter—to recognize God and all things. 

This is just as the **Trinity** <note>original: *Trinitet*</note> is twofold: both internal-eternal and external-temporal. Through such study, every person should be brought back to themselves, to see how everything we learn is already within us, and that there is no Trinity outside of man. Thus, the **Holy Spirit** should be correctly recognized as the third Person [of the Trinity], which is the person themselves; for we are not without God, and God is not without us.

These figures below indicate the **crucifixion under-work** <note>original: *Creußigung vnderwerk*; likely referring to the practical or structural process of "crucifying" the text to extract meaning</note> of the Word and Spirit. It moves many of the **30 paragraphs** <note>original: *30. Paragraphos*</note> into other chapters (for it is a breaking of the paragraphs through the three crosses), and they are taken down as part of the under-work.

There is, however, a twofold crucifixion: one of the Spirit along with the Word forward, and a crucifixion of the Word along with the Spirit downward. The crucifixion of the Spirit forward belongs only to the chapters of the whole Apocalypse <note>original Latin: *totius Apocalypseos*; the Book of Revelation</note>; even if one takes only the letters and spirits of the paragraphs <note>original Latin: *Paragraphorum*</note> and hangs them on the three crosses, they remain only the chapters. 

(Since in the Great Work <note>The *Magnum Opus*, or the process of spiritual perfection</note> all chapters of the Bibles <note>original Latin: *Capita Bibliorum*</note> belong to the Spirit, and all paragraphs <note>original Latin: *Paragraphi*</note> belong to the Word.) 

The crucifixion of the spirits along with the Word forward indicates only the 11 chapters of the Spirit, and the fact that they are a grave—that is, that the Apocalypse <note>original Latin: *Apocalypsis*</note> is a grave, into which the 20 paragraphs <note>original Latin: *Paragraphi*</note> are crucified downward, and broken, taken down, and lowered toward the Resurrection.

Likewise, continue by taking from the points <note>original: "Pu-", likely "Puncten" (points)</note> of the three crosses q, r, s, which provides chapters 12, 13, and 14 with the [yellow] field; T, V, X the blue color; y, z, h the red; and ζ, 9, t the [green]. Thus, the Trinity <note>original: "Trinitet"</note> reveals itself through the Persons as being twofold: internal and eternal (Spirit, Person, Word) and external and temporal (Word, Person, Spirit). This is the Revelation <note>original: "Offenbahhrung"</note> of Jesus Christ (stated according to the Spirit and Word).

...at the three crosses/three times four makes 12. The **Father** with the twelve sons of Israel at the first cross/the **Son** with the twelve stars at the middle cross/the **Holy Spirit** with the twelve apostles at the third cross. There are thus twelve persons, according to the three times four points; for in each of the four points <note>original: "in quolibet quatuor punctorum"</note> two letters come together from the Spirit and the Word, and produce one person. Thus, a person hangs at each point, for a person is not without God and Word, nor can God and Word exist without a person. These three [as] one God cannot be separated, neither in the heavenly nor the earthly kingdom, any more than eternity and time.

But time and eternity—that is, the heavenly and earthly kingdoms—are both within man. Thus there are 12 persons according to the 12 stars that are found in the three crosses: the Father is fourfold and has 4; the Son has 8; the Holy Spirit [has] 12, who encompasses the entire world.

Fifthly, we see from this that the two suns belong to the cross of the Father—the sun is the Spirit or yellow color—and the 12 stars belong to the cross of the Son—the star represents the person—and the 12 moons belong to the third cross of the Holy Spirit—the moon signifies the Word, which became flesh in the world.

Sixthly, such an aforementioned crucifixion points us to the four times 6 numbers, according to the four points of the cross, with the four Evangelists <note>The four authors of the Gospels: Matthew, Mark, Luke, and John.</note> together with their 24 wings <note>In mystical tradition and the Book of Revelation, the four "living creatures" (associated with the Evangelists) each have six wings, totaling twenty-four.</note>, which occupy the 12 hours of the day—as **Matthew** and **Mark**, and the first twelve chapters: Likewise, those that occupy the 12 hours of the night—as **Luke** and **John**, and also the other 12 chapters of the Word, including the enclosure of the two half-rainbows with their four colors.

# 87.
## J 3.

From the Unbegotten <note>original: "ex Ingenito"; a theological term for God the Father, who is not created or begotten by any other.</note>, in Him one finds Heaven, Hell, and all places <note>original: "Loca"</note>, etc., for He is the Incomprehensible Infinity <note>original: "Infinitum incomprehensibile"</note>. In Him one finds all times, for He is the Eternal <note>original: "Æternum"</note>. Therefore He is all hours, all days, all weeks, all months, all years, all feast days, all ages <note>original: "Secula"</note>, and times. He has the seven stars in His hand, the twelve celestial signs <note>original: "Signa Cœlestia"; referring to the constellations of the Zodiac</note>, and the seven ages of the world; everything is from Him, and through Him, and all things must return into Him. If you seek the Trinity <note>original: "Trinitet"</note> outside of Christ, you will not find it; if you seek time, day, hour, or any book outside of Him, you will find nothing. If you seek any art or wisdom in the heavenly or earthly light outside of Christ, you will find nothing. He remains the Great Book, Heaven and Earth, for Heaven and Earth came to be through Him; therefore He carries all of that within Himself, and it is written both within and without. One finds and learns everything from Him, nothing excluded. From this you might well understand what the scholars <note>original: "Literati"</note> say: *He who studies without a Book, draws water with a sieve* <note>original: "Qui studet sine Libro, haurit Aquam sine Cribro"; a Latin proverb suggesting that academic study without spiritual foundation is a futile, leaky effort.</note>. As they all testify among themselves, they deal with mere air all their lives and can reach no end, because they study without the Book.

Everything depends on the two images of God and Word, which flow together into the Person, inwardly invisible; this Person is God and Word, become flesh, born as man. Thus the Trinity <note>original: "Trinitet"</note> has become physical and temporal, yet has remained heavenly and invisible—wholly in Heaven, wholly on Earth, wholly in man. In Heaven is God the Father, and He is threefold; in Christ is God the Son, and He is threefold; in man is God the Holy Spirit, and He is threefold. In short <note>original: "in Summa"</note>, these two images encompass all things.

<term>Complica librum</term> <note>original Latin: "Fold the book"; a command to close or fold the metaphorical book of nature/scripture to see how its parts align.</note>, fold it together like a book; thus the Moon and Saturn come together, and Mars and Venus also come together. ## On the Establishment of the Great or Whole Work.

The Great Work <note>The *Magnum Opus*, or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> would be made through the crucifixion of the verses <note>original Latin: *Versus*</note>, where one crucifies the chapters through the three spirits <note>original: *Gester*</note> of the 6 chapters; thus the grave emerges out of the three points of the three crosses with the four colors of the rainbows <note>original Latin: *Iridum*</note>. Just as the first three points, such as **a. b. c.**, have yellow and set the first three chapters to the grave, the other three points of the cross arrange the subsequent three chapters—namely 4, 5, and 6—into the blue field, through the removal of the letters from the blue points **d. e. f.** The third points from the red color arrange the chapters into the red field: **g. h. i.** The three green points arrange chapters 10, 11, and 12 into the green field through **k. l. m.** There you have the 11 chapters <note>The text specifies 11 chapters here, though the sequence implies 12; this may refer to a specific spiritual grouping.</note> of the Spirit, which are arranged through the crucifixion before the grave, into which the 30 paragraphs <note>original Latin: *Paragraphi*</note> and the whole Scripture must be laid with Christ. However, such chapters could not be crucified if one did not have the 30 paragraphs of the 6 chapters; and although such crucifixion of the chapters through the spirits or letters of the paragraphs brings no explanation or interpretation, the grave could not be arranged without such a preceding crucifixion with the three times and four colors of the rainbow.

Thus, take away further the letters from the three points—yellow, blue, red, green—and so the other eleven chapters are arranged into their places <note>original Latin: *Loca*</note>, as the letters testify:

# KNOW
## THYSELF.
<note>original Greek: "GNOTHI SEAUTON"; a famous ancient maxim emphasizing that self-knowledge is the foundation of all wisdom.</note>

Whoever studies the <term>Apocalypse<note>The Book of Revelation; the final book of the New Testament, known for its complex symbolism.</note></term>, that is, <term>J.C.G.M.<note>original: "I. C. G. M."; likely an abbreviation for "Jesus Christus Gottes Mensch" (Jesus Christ, God-Man), a central concept in the author's mystical theology.</note></term>, he studies and learns nothing else but that from which he is made and taken, and that which he has and carries upon himself, and that which he himself is. J.C.G.M. is our <term>clay<note>original Latin: "Limus"; referring to the "dust of the ground" from which God formed man in Genesis 2:7.</note></term>; we are from Him internally and externally. Therefore, Christ is in us, and we are in Him; we both are one. J.C.G.M. is all Books, for He is ever the eighth among the seven lampstands; He is also all the <term>Fathers<note>original: "Vätter"; likely referring to the Patriarchs or the early Church Fathers who provide the spiritual lineage of faith.</note></term>, for He is the eighth among the seven stars. Thus Christ encompasses 77 Fathers and 77 Books, as well as Heaven and Earth, and all creatures within them. From all this, man is made and taken; therefore, he must be in the same, and have and carry the same within himself, just as Christ does. <note>original Latin: "Quo cognito, omnia sunt cognita in cælo & in terra."</note> *Once this is known, all things are known in heaven and on earth.*

But so that one may recognize J.C. and oneself, and all things in heaven and earth, then take before you the two images in the *Apocalypse*: God and Word. In this you have the first three *Chapters* with their 15 *Paragraphs*—that is, Spirit or God—and the 12th, 13th, and 14th *Chapters* with their 15 *Paragraphs*—that is, Word. Indeed, every *Chapter* and *Paragraph* has its <term>immovable letters<note>original: "vnuerzückliche Buchstaben"; suggesting that the very letters of the text contain fixed, spiritual meanings that do not change.</note></term> and spirits, through which the entire Scripture is governed and guided, explained and opened, along with the **Holy Trinity**, and everything that is created and made in heaven and earth.

# KNOW 
## THYSELF.<note>original Greek: "GNOTHI SEAUTON."</note>

Whoever studies the <term>Apocalypse<note>original: "Apocalypsin"; the Book of Revelation. In this mystical context, it is treated as a map of the human soul rather than just a prophecy of future events.</note>—that is, Jesus Christ, God and Man <note>original: "I. C. G. M."; likely an abbreviation for *Jesus Christus Gott Mensch*.</note>—he studies and learns nothing other than that from which he was made and taken, and that which he has and carries within himself, and that which he himself is. Jesus Christ, God and Man, is our <term>clay<note>original: "Limus"; referring to the "dust of the ground" or primordial slime from which God formed man in Genesis 2:7.</note>.</note> We are from Him, both inwardly and outwardly; therefore, Christ is in us, and we are in Him; we both are one. Jesus Christ, God and Man, is all books, for He is indeed the eighth among the seven <term>candlesticks<note>original: "Leuchtern"; referring to the seven golden lampstands in Revelation 1:12-13, representing the churches, with Christ standing in their midst.</note>.</note> He is also all the Fathers, for He is the eighth among the seven stars. Thus, Christ encompasses seventy-seven fathers and seventy-seven books, as well as heaven and earth, and all creatures within them. Out of all this, Man is made and taken; therefore, he must be in the same, and have and carry the same within himself, just as Christ does. *When this is known, all things in heaven and on earth are known.*<note>original Latin: "Quo cognito, omnia sunt cognita in cœlo & in terra."</note>

But so that one may recognize Jesus Christ and oneself, and all things in heaven and on earth, take before you the two images in the Apocalypse: God and the Word. Thus you have the first three chapters with their fifteen paragraphs—that is, the Spirit or God—and chapters 12, 13, and 14 with their fifteen paragraphs—that is, the Word. Indeed, every chapter and paragraph has its unmovable letters and spirits through which the entire Scripture is governed and guided, explained and opened, along with the Holy <term>Trinity<note>original: "Trinitet."</note></term> and everything that is created and made in heaven and on earth.

The crucifixion <note>original: *creutzigung*; used here to mean a cross-wise mapping or systematic arrangement</note> of the subjection <note>original: *vnderwerff*; likely referring to the way one set of concepts is "laid under" or mapped onto another</note> of the Word and Spirit retains all: Words, Spirits, Paragraphs, Members, Books, and Fathers <note>Spiritual authorities or source texts</note>, as it has been from eternity and must still be. It is the crucifixion of subjection when each Chapter of the Spirit, with its 5 Paragraphs, clothes and occupies the 5 Points of the first Cross, as **a. b. c. d. e.**

The second Chapter, with its 5 Paragraphs, occupies and clothes the 5 points of the Middle Cross **f. g. h. i. k.**

The third Chapter, with its 5 Paragraphs, occupies and clothes the 5 Points of the third Cross: **l. m. n. o. p.** There you have hands and feet, together with the 5 wounds of the inner person who is heavenly and is named Jesus.

Likewise, in the Word, the first 5 Paragraphs occupy the 12th Chapter—the first cross: **q. r. s. t. u.** The following paragraphs from the 12th and 13th Chapters clothe once again the middle cross: **x. y. z. ћ. ξ.** <note>The author uses the symbols for Saturn and the Greek letter Xi</note> And the Paragraphs of the 14th Chapter occupy once again the third cross: **9 t u v 8.**

Now you must take for yourself from the cross the first three points: **a. f. l.** Thus, these paragraphs fall into the 12th, 13th, and 14th Chapters as if into their grave, to declare themselves and to also explain such Chapters through the Fathers and Books.

**B. G. M.** paragraphs fall into the 4th, 5th, and 6th Chapters, so that they may declare and be declared, explain and be explained <note>original Latin: *ut declarent & declarentur, explicent & explicentur*</note>.

**C. h. n.** paragraphs—Fathers, Books, Members, Joints <note>original: *Glieder/ Gelencke*; metaphors for the interlocking parts of the spiritual system</note>—fall into the 7th, 8th, and 9th Chapters, so that they may declare and be declared <note>original Latin: *vt declarent & declarentur*</note>.

**D. i. o.** paragraphs—Spirits, Fathers, Books, Members, Joints—fall into the 10th, 11th, and 12th, so that they may declare and be declared <note>original Latin: *vt declarent & declaren-tur*</note>.

**E. k. p.** paragraphs—Fathers, Books, Members, Joints—fall into the 12th, 13th, and 14th Chapters.

Thus, take the Word from the Cross.

**ρ. x. 9.** Paragraphs—Books, Fathers, Limbs—fall into the first 23rd Chapter.

### III.

The crucifixion <note>In this context, "crucifixion" refers to the symbolic mapping of text onto the geometric structure of a cross, a common practice in 17th-century mystical diagrams</note> of the sub-work <note>original German: *vnderwerck*; referring to a secondary or manifest operation of the divine</note> of the Word and Spirit preserves everything: Words, Spirits, Paragraphs, Members, Books, and Fathers, as it has been from eternity and must still be. This "sub-work crucifixion" occurs when each Chapter of the Spirit, with its 5 paragraphs, clothes and occupies the 5 points of the first cross, namely **a. b. c. d. e.** The second Chapter, with its 5 paragraphs, occupies and clothes the 5 points of the Middle Cross: **f. g. h. i. k.**

The third Chapter, with its 5 paragraphs, occupies and clothes the 5 points of the third cross: **l. m. n. o. p.** There you have the hands and feet, together with the 5 wounds of the internal person <note>The "inner person" (*innerliche Person*) is a concept in German mysticism referring to the spiritual soul that mirrors the celestial body of Christ</note> who is heavenly and is named Jesus.

Likewise in the Word, the first 5 paragraphs occupy the 12th Chapter [of] the first cross: **q. r. s. t. u.** The following paragraphs from the 12th and 13th Chapters clothe once again the middle cross: **x. y. z. h. ξ.** <note>The author uses the Greek letter xi (ξ) as a coordinate</note> And the paragraphs of the 14th Chapter occupy once again the third cross: **9 t u 8.**

Now, you must first take the first three points from the cross: **a. f. l.** These paragraphs thus fall into the 12th, 13th, and 14th Chapters as if into their grave, to explain themselves, and also to explain such Chapters through the Fathers and Books.

**B. G. M.** paragraphs fall into the 4th, 5th, and 6th Chapters, so that they may declare and be declared, and explain and be explained <note>original Latin: *ut declarent & declarentur, explicent & explicentur*</note>.

**C. h. n.** paragraphs—Fathers, Books, Members, and Joints—fall into the 7th, 8th, and 9th Chapters, so that they may declare and be declared <note>original Latin: *ut declarent & declarentur*</note>.

**D. i. o.** paragraphs—Spirits, Fathers, Books, Members, and Joints—fall into the 10th, 11th, and 12th [Chapters], so that they may declare and be declared.

**E. k. p.** paragraphs—Fathers, Books, Members, and Joints—fall into the 12th, 13th, and 14th Chapters.

Thus, take the Word from the cross.

**e. x. 9.** paragraphs—Books, Fathers, and Limbs—fall into the first 23rd Chapter.

The Sun wards us off by being too hot and fiery, and the Moon by being too cold and dark; if the Earth is to benefit from them, it must occur through the Constellations <note>The author suggests that the "Stars" or "Gestirn" act as mediators, tempering the extreme qualities of the sun and moon so the earth can survive them.</note>.

These two unite into the Person who is heavenly, and this Person gives itself forth through Creation and becomes flesh.

# Of the Right True Understanding
## of the Trinity, and of all things in Heaven and Earth.

If anyone wishes to study and recognize True Theology <note>original Latin: *Veram Theologiam*</note>, that is, all heavenly and earthly wisdom, he must before all things rightly recognize the Trinity. That is, he must recognize the Beginning, Middle, and End, but primarily learn to unite the End with its Beginning.

We find in True Theology <note>original Latin: *Vera Theologia*</note> that the Tetraktys <note>original Greek: $\tau\epsilon\tau\rho\acute{\alpha}\kappa\tau\upsilon\varsigma$; a mystical Pythagorean symbol of ten points arranged in a triangle, representing the harmony of the universe.</note> and the Ten <note>original Latin: *denarius*</note> are the Universal, in which 28 is found <note>The number 28 is a "perfect number" and the sum of the first seven integers (1+2+3+4+5+6+7).</note>, which leads back to 1, the One <note>original Latin: *Vnum*</note>. Likewise, the Three <note>original Latin: *Ternarius*</note> is found in 45 <note>The number 45 is the sum of the first nine integers (1 through 9).</note>. The Seven <note>original Latin: *Septenarius*</note> and the Eight <note>original Latin: *Octonarius*</note> are in 10. No one can go further; for 11, 12, 20, 30, 500, and 1,000 must all return into the One <note>original Latin: *Vnum*</note>.

The Theological Tetraktys is Spirit / Person / Word / Humanity; that is, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit is God <note>original: *v. s. h. G. ist G.*, an abbreviation for *Vater, Sohn, heiliger Geist ist Gott*.</note>. That is the Theological Four <note>original Latin: *Quaternarius Theologicus*</note>. Within it, you find all creatures in Heaven and Earth: Astronomy, Philosophy, Physics, Alchemy, Magic, Medicine, Jurisprudence, etc.

Even if you wanted to study arts and languages, you could not find them outside of this divine Tetraktys <note>original Greek: $\tau\epsilon\tau\rho\acute{\alpha}\kappa\tau\upsilon\nu$</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Trinity from page 117, shifting from mathematical symbols (like the Tetraktys and the number 45) to the spiritual source of all knowledge. The author argues that true wisdom does not come from academic study of languages or the liberal arts, but from the eternal "Center" or the "Unbegotten," using the mystic Paul Lautensack as a primary reference point.</meta>

118.

But for now, I let the arts and languages rest, as secondary matters or, as it were, external additions <note>original Latin: *parerga, vel Aduentitia quasi*</note>, and I hasten only toward the real things, toward the center <note>original Latin: *ad Realia ad centripusum*</note>. For we know that the highest and most learned theologians, mages, philosophers, alchemists, physicians, and lawyers <note>original Latin: *Theologi, Magi, Philosophi, Alchymistæ, Medici, Iurisperiti*</note> can exist without the "trivial and quadrivial arts" <note>The *Artes triviales* (grammar, logic, rhetoric) and *quadriviales* (arithmetic, geometry, music, astronomy) were the seven liberal arts forming the standard medieval university curriculum.</note> and without languages.

This is because every person can attain the Universal <note>The absolute or foundational truth of the universe.</note> in his own mother tongue. Not that I reject the arts and languages—any more than I would cast off a man's fingernails or toenails, the hair on his head or beard, or his ears—but rather I lead toward true theology <note>original Latin: *ad veram Theologiam*</note>, that is, toward heavenly and earthly wisdom, which can certainly exist without languages and arts, as you yourself must confess.

Now ask Paul Lautensack <note>Paul Lautensack (1491–1558) was a German painter and organist who became a visionary mystic; his works used complex diagrams to explain the nature of God and the Trinity.</note> from where he takes these four words: Spirit, Person, Word, Humanity.

->Answer:<-

They come from that which remains without beginning and eternal. For the beginning, middle, and end of all things come from the unbegun; all created things <note>original Latin: *Genita*</note> from the uncreated <note>original Latin: *Ingenito*</note>, all produced things from the unproduced. This essence is the One Infinite and Eternal <note>original Latin: *Vnum Infinitum, æternum*</note>, that is, the Monad—meaning the Unity, the Center, or the Point <note>original Latin: *Monas i. e. Vnitas, Centrum seu Punctum*</note>—from which all things come and into which they must return. This uncreated thing or Center is a source, well, and origin of all things in heaven and on earth. It is in all, through all, and above all; entirely within and entirely without; eternal, invisible, incomprehensible, and unsearchable. It is the beginning without beginning, the middle, and the end of all things, from which comes the Trinity: Father, Son, Holy Spirit, Man, and the World.

Now, to demonstrate to the mind's eye <note>original Latin: *ad oculum Mentalem*</note> that all things spring from the Center or the eternal uncreated, know that that which has a beginning or origin does not have its beginning from itself, but from that which remains eternal and without beginning. That is called, and is in Hebrew, YHWH <note>original Hebrew: יהוה; the Tetragrammaton or the four-letter name of God.</note>.

119.

First without the First; for He is the **One** <note>original Latin: *Vnarius*</note>, He is the **Two** <note>original Latin: *Binarius*</note>, He is the **Three** <note>original Latin: *Ternarius*</note>, He is the **Four** <note>original Latin: *Quaternarius*</note>, as the four letters <note>Likely referring to the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God (YHWH).</note> testify. It is an origin of the **Five, Seven, Eight, Ten,** etc. <note>original Latin: *Quinarius, Septenarius, Octonarius, Denarius*</note>. Within this lies hidden: Spirit, Person, Word, Humanity; the heavenly and earthly **Tetraktys** <note>original Greek: *τετρακτυς*. In Pythagorean mysticism, this is a triangular figure consisting of ten points, representing the organization of the universe.</note>.

The word **Spirit** <note>original German: *Geist*. The author proceeds to use the five letters of the German word—G, E, I, S, T—to explain the divine structure.</note> has five letters. The first, **G**, signifies to us the **One Unbegotten, Infinite** <note>original Latin: *Vnum Ingenitum, Infinitum*</note>. The second, the **E**, signifies the Son; the **Begotten Word** <note>original Latin: *Genitum*</note>, which was in the beginning and has a beginning. The third, the **I**, signifies the Person. The fourth, the **S**, signifies the Son of God—the Father in the Son. The fifth, the **T**, signifies Humanity, the Holy Spirit, the whole world, the outflow and origin of the three heavenly and temporal persons (for they have a beginning).

There you have two, and yet it is only one Godhead, divided only in Person. These two, as **GOD** and **WORD**, are not without Person, but must be united together to give one Person, which is the Son of God inwardly;

120.

For when God and the Word come together, they produce a Person; and that is the Father in the Son. There you have the Father and Son in the heavenly essence <note>original German: *wesen*. This refers to the fundamental nature or "being" of the divine.</note>, and you have them both with a beginning; thus the Father is a child of Mary, because He is the Father in the Son. <note>The author presents a mystical paradox: because the Father and Son are inseparable, the Father is "born" into the world through the Son's incarnation via the Virgin Mary.</note>

Thus you have Spirit, Person, Word: the Spirit from which the Word comes, and the Person who holds the Spirit and Word within themselves—otherwise, it would not be a Person. This Person, after the creation of Heaven and Earth, became visible and earthly; that is, the Word became flesh, and God was born as a human. Therefore, the three divine Persons are essentially and truly present in humanity: as Spirit, Person, and Word internally, and as Word, Person, and Spirit externally.

Behold, from the Unbegotten <note>original Latin: *Ingenitum*. This refers to the ultimate source of God that was not created or "begotten" by anything else.</note> there comes forth Spirit, Person, Word, and Humanity. The Spirit is Jehovah Unbegotten <note>original Latin/German: *Iehoua Ingenitum*</note>; the Person arises from the Word and the Spirit and is the Father in the Son; this Word is the Son of God, the Son in the Father; and Humanity <note>original German: *Menschheit*</note> is the Holy Spirit, the whole world.

121.

So that you need not wonder how and why the <term>Eternal Unbegotten</term> <note>original Latin: *Ingenitum æternum*. This refers to the ultimate, uncaused source of God.</note> could bring forth from itself the <term>begotten</term> <note>original Latin: *progenitum*</note> Word, and out of itself and the Word the begotten Person, and thereafter from these three the outward Person, Christ—who is nonetheless to be recognized both internally and externally, for in Him the <term>Trinity</term> <note>original German: *Trinitet*</note> reveals itself both heavenly and earthly. Know then that <term>like begets its like</term> <note>original Latin: *Simile generare suum Simile*</note>, and that God makes nothing (Take Note) except what He Himself is, and that this does not happen without a Person. If the <term>Infinite Unbegotten</term> <note>original Latin: *Ingenitum Infinitum*</note> were to bring forth an <term>Eternal Good</term> <note>original Latin: *æternum Bonum*</note>, a Word with a beginning, then He Himself must be a Person <term>in eternity</term> <note>original Latin: *in æterno*</note>; that is, He must have a Word and be a Spirit, for Word and Spirit are not found without a Person, and the Spirit and the Word can do nothing without a Person. Thus the <term>Unbegotten</term> is in eternity nothing other than Spirit, Person, and Word—it is threefold. If God is a Spirit <term>in eternity</term> <note>original Latin: *in æternitate*</note>, then He is not without the Word; if He is Word and Spirit, then a Person is necessary so that He could bring forth the Word from Himself. But if He is threefold and possesses an <term>Eternal Threefoldness</term> <note>original Latin: *Ternarium æternum*</note> without beginning, then He is the <term>Tetrad</term> <note>original Greek: *τετράκτυς* (tetraktys). A Pythagorean term for the number four, here used to describe the full, manifest structure of the Divine.</note>, the <term>Unbegotten</term>, the <term>Infinite Sea</term> <note>original Latin: *Mare infinitum*</note> from which all things have arisen. His name is <term>ineffable</term> <note>original Latin: *ineffabile*; unutterable</note>, His essence unsearchable; yet among us He is called **יהוה** <note>The Hebrew Tetragrammaton, YHWH, the sacred name of God.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Christ as the cosmic center of all knowledge. While the previous page categorized earthly disciplines (like Alchemy and Medicine) as branches of the knowledge of Christ, this page delves into the ontological structure of the Word and the containment of all creation—both spiritual and physical—within the person of Jesus Christ.</meta>

125.

Thus the Begotten Word <note>original: *Genitum Verbum*</note> is Word, Person, and Spirit, and each of these is in turn threefold.
Now from the unbegotten spirit and the begotten Word <note>original: *ex ingenito spiritum, & genito Verbo*</note>, these two make one Day, one Person; this Person from God and the Word is in turn Person, Word, and Spirit—threefold—and each of these is again threefold. Humanity <note>original: *Menschheit*</note> is also ninefold, for within it are three Heavenly Persons.
All things that have been called forth out of the hiddenness and darkness into the light, into the day, they are all made and created by God and the Word in the Person; that is, through Jesus Christ <note>original: *Christum Jesum*</note> all things have come to be in Heaven and on Earth, and without him nothing is made that is made. He is the firstborn of all creatures <note>original: *Creaturen*</note>; He is eternal with God; in Him is the Spirit and the Word; and He also finally became visible at the end of the world <note>original: *in fine Mundi*</note>, born as a man. This means: all things that were eternal in God have been born into the world as a man through the Son—visible, bodily, and tangible. Just as all things in Heaven and on Earth are created and made out of Him, from Him, and through Him, so too do all creatures, along with Heaven and Earth, remain enclosed and contained within Him.
All things consist more in Jesus Christ than in themselves <note>original: *Omnia magis consistunt in I. C. quam in seipsis*</note>. He made the angels, therefore all angels and devils must remain enclosed and contained within Him. He is the essence and life of all angels and devils. He made the heavens, therefore Heaven and Hell must remain enclosed and contained within Him. He made this visible world, Heaven and Earth, and the four elements <note>original: *Elementen*</note>; therefore, this entire world must remain enclosed and contained within Him as if it were a single point. He holds the seven stars <note>original: *Sternen*</note> in his hand; He is the eighth among the seven.

He is the Beginning, from Him who has no beginning. <unclear>at</unclear>

The Spirit or Deity has no beginning, and gives a beginning to all created things; <note>original: "Crenitis," likely a scribe's error for "Creatis" (created things).</note> and with them He also makes Himself the beginning, and yet remains without beginning. Whatever gives a beginning to all origins must have no beginning for itself, lest there be a process into infinity <note>original: "ne fiat Processus in infinitum," a philosophical argument that there cannot be an endless chain of causes; there must be a First Cause.</note>; and that is the Spirit who has contained within Himself the Father, Son, Holy Spirit, and the Word or Humanity, and all creatures. Thus the Spirit is now the Heaven and the Hell; that is, outside of Him there is neither Heaven nor Hell, neither light nor darkness, neither salvation nor damnation, neither angel nor devil; in Him we live, move, and have our being, <note>original: "in ipso viuimus, mouemur & sumus," a reference to Acts 17:28.</note> just as the Spirit encloses all things as Center and Circle <note>original: "Centrum vnd Circulus"</note>. M.," standing for Iesus Christus Gott Mensch</note> also the Center and Circle, and encloses and encompasses Heaven, Hell, angels, devils, day and night; and all creatures in Heaven and on Earth are enclosed and contained within Him. Just as they were not created without Him, so also can they not be known or understood without Him. He is the seven seals, which do not only seal and lock the seven books or the Apocalypse <note>original: "Apocalypsin," the Book of Revelation</note>, but also Heaven and Earth, Hell and all creatures. If anyone wishes to study and know the four elements <note>original: "Elementa"</note>, he must study Christ, the seven seals, for He is a natural philosopher <note>original: "Philosophus physicus"</note>, a fact which the world neither wants to know nor believe. If anyone wishes to study the heavens and the stars, he must recognize and teach Christ with the seven stars; thus He is the perfect astronomer <note>original: "perfectus Astronomus"</note>, which the worldly ones <note>original: "Mundani"</note> do not believe; for He who is the maker and creator of all things can teach us best.

<page-num>133.</page-num>
<header>OF THE SECRET OF ALL SECRETS.</header>
<sig>R iij</sig>
<meta>catchword: speak.</meta>

# Of the Greatest Secret of all
# Secrets in Heaven and
# Earth.

There is only one thing or essence, from which the heavenly and earthly kingdoms spring and flow; indeed, that same essence is without beginning, and yet has a beginning. It is unborn and also born; from Him, out of Him, through Him are all things. From this single thing or essence is recognized and revealed the true eternal God, the First and the Last, the Beginning and the End, with two kingdoms—with the heavenly and the earthly kingdom. Both are in this world: The heavenly is not *outside the world* <note>original Latin: "extra Mundum"</note> above the spheres, but in the world (I say) is the heavenly kingdom, within which the three divine Persons <note>The Holy Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.</note> reign: *Matthew 20*. The heavenly kingdom is like a King, a vineyard; our conduct is in heaven; we seek what is above, where Christ is at the right hand of the Father; and the kingdom of heaven, *our homeland* <note>original Latin: "Patria nostra"</note>, is not *bound to a place* <note>original Latin: "Locale"</note>, though it is indeed in the world, etc. What goods the faithful possess in this kingdom of heaven, both in this and the future life, you may read in the Scriptures.

The earthly kingdom is also not outside that single essence, and is also in this world, such as the four monarchies <note>Refers to the traditional "Four World Monarchies" of Daniel's prophecy: usually Babylon, Persia, Greece, and Rome.</note> together with the Jewish kingdom: those of Nebuchadnezzar, Cyrus, Alexander the Great, Augustus, Charlemagne, Charles V, and Rudolph <note>Likely Rudolf II, Holy Roman Emperor (reigned 1576–1612), suggesting the text was written during or shortly after his time.</note>. All dominions, all peoples, heathens, and languages are upon Earth and govern in, with, and through the One, of whom I

<summary>This page introduces the "Secret of all Secrets," describing a single, eternal essence from which both the heavenly and earthly kingdoms originate. It argues that the kingdom of heaven is not a distant location above the stars but a spiritual reality present within the world.</summary>
<keywords>divine essence, heavenly kingdom, earthly kingdom, four monarchies, Holy Trinity, Holy Roman Emperors, omnipresence of God</keywords>

speech. We pray:
> Thy will be done, as in heaven so also on earth;
in this, the two kingdoms <note>The heavenly and earthly kingdoms discussed on the previous page.</note> are enclosed and contained within the single Essence. Therefore, in this <term>earthly kingdom</term> <note>original: "jrzdiſchen Reiche"</note>, the three bodily, earthly Persons reveal themselves—Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—as one Christ. This single Essence is the seat and throne of the Holy Trinity, and a container and concept of Heaven and Earth and all creatures.

All things have come to be in, out of, and through the single † Essence, and are enclosed and contained by the same. It is one and three, and yet four; yes, it is seven and eight; it is 36, 666, etc. <note>These numbers refer to mystical numerology common in theosophy: 1 (The One), 3 (Trinity), 4 (Elements/Cross), 7 (Planetary spirits/Creation), and 666 (the number of the Beast, but here likely used to show the Essence contains even the furthest extremes of the material/fallen world).</note> It is a container and concept of all essences, penetrating all things, existing in all things, and yet eternal and incomprehensible. If someone seeks it, it allows itself to be found everywhere, but not grasped. If someone wishes to enjoy it, they can never possess or grasp it from the outside—<note>original Latin: "extra se"</note> outside of oneself—but only within yourself can you find and enjoy it, for it is your Light and Life:
> Nothing is greater, more spacious, or more powerful than this, because all things are in it; nothing is smaller or more subtle than it, because it is in all the smallest things. <note>original Latin: "Hoc nihil maius, capacius, potentius, quia omnia ſunt in ipſo; Eo nihil minus. ſubtilius, quia eſt in omnibus Minimis."</note>

All things were made through the same; in Him is life, and the life is the light of men, and the light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not understood it; it enlightens all men who come into this world. It is before all, over all, through all, and in all. This very one is the Word that became flesh in the world; what was eternal inwardly, He has become temporally outwardly: a throne, seat, and stool of the Majesty of GOD. He is the Beginning and the Origin of Heaven and Earth, for He is Alpha and Omega. <note>original Greek: "α vnd ω"</note> He is all-penetrating and all-searching, for His face shines like the sun and He has eyes like flames of fire. He encloses and contains Heaven and Earth, for He has seven stars in His hand and stands within the entire rainbow. <note>These images are drawn from the Book of Revelation (Apocalypse), describing the glorified Christ.</note> He reigns over all nations

136.

I must not write explicitly about this Secret of Secrets <note>original: "Arcanum Arcanorum"; a term used in alchemy and mysticism for the highest hidden truth.</note>; God would punish me for it.

Man is created simple and upright: but through sheer self-will, he seeks out many arts; he should have known neither good nor evil, nor eaten of them, but he forgets his Creator and Redeemer; he wants to know much about God and created things, yet refuses to see the Secret of Secrets ✝
<image-desc>A circular diagram containing a central vertical line and a horizontal line forming a cross. The right side of the circle is bounded by a curved line, resembling a capital letter 'D' or a bow.</image-desc>
<margin>No man can teach this of himself; GOD teaches it to him.</margin>

The highest Secret of Secrets is the Cross ✝ from which all heavenly and earthly wisdom is taught and recognized; whoever teaches outside of this Cross ✝ is deceived and led astray by the serpent, such that he does not recognize the dragon, that ancient serpent. An example of how the serpent deceives us:

In our instruction, we do not recognize the <term>highest good</term><note>original: "summum Bonum"</note> in a Christian way (NB), but in a heathen manner with <term>Palingenius</term><note>Refers to Marcello Palingenio Stellato, a 16th-century poet whose "Zodiacus Vitae" was a famous blend of science, philosophy, and theology, though sometimes criticized for its pagan intellectualism.</note>, as we see: God is eternal, without beginning; the <term>creature</term><note>original: "Creatur"; refers to any created being or thing.</note> is created with a beginning. God is <term>without need</term>, <term>without affect</term><note>unmoved by external passions or emotions.</note>, <term>without will</term>, <term>without desire</term>, <term>without action</term>. [People] begin to think and speculate how the eternal, inactive Good could move itself in its eternity to act, to will, to create; thus they separate the <term>Alpha and Omega</term><note>The beginning and the end.</note>, beginning and end, eternity and time, God and creature from one another. They presume that the eternal GOD by Himself is entirely without the temporal creature, and the temporal creature is entirely without God, and that it was created so that it should have a free will, and should know, see, and recognize, and act or refrain by its own power. They say thus: God is without affect, without will, and needs nothing, for eternally <term>He desires nothing</term><note>original: "nihil desiderat"</note>. Therefore, He created the rational creature <term>perfectly</term><note>original: "perfectè"</note>, so that it could or should will, know, work, and think for itself—not [realizing] that an image cannot exist without the essence. Thus the creature falls

...turns back to itself; she thinks she is the one; she believes she is capable of and does everything herself; she makes herself into a God and follows the serpent, who speaks: "Ah, should God have forbidden that? Do you not see that the Highest is without need, works nothing, does nothing, wills nothing, but has surrendered everything to you—the will, the knowledge, the power, the doing, the letting be, etc.? And you should also be like God."

When the end is thus separated from the beginning (which is nevertheless impossible), then the <term>creature</term><note>original: "Creatur"; here referring to the human being as a created entity.</note> is deceived, for she forgets the <term>Secret of Secrets</term><note>original: "Arcanum Arcanorum"; a mystical term for the ultimate hidden truth.</note>, ✝ the Stone for the heavenly building, which must yet remain the cornerstone.

Now observe through your clear reason: you see what God is in eternity, but you forget what God is in time with eternity ✝ namely the <term>Alpha and Omega</term><note>original Greek letters: "α vnd ω"; meaning the beginning and the end.</note>. These must remain together, for nothing is whole, and nothing is blessed, without the <term>union of opposites</term><note>original Latin: "Combinatione Contradictoriorum"; a philosophical and mystical concept that truth resides in the meeting of seemingly contradictory forces.</note>. If you can now see and know what GOD works, wills, and does in eternity, then you should truly also not forget that He wills, works, and does in Time with eternity—and that He is not only eternally heavenly, but also temporal and earthly; that the Earth is His just as much as Heaven, and that He is a God over Heaven and Earth.

Through such speculating, thinking, knowing, and arguing, you forget that you possess nothing of yourself, and that you did not create yourself. You imagine the end exists without the beginning, time without eternity, and the creature without God—which is yet impossible. Even as you see, speculate, and recognize what God is in eternity, you testify by your own self that God works, knows, and wills in, with, and through you, and that you think neither evil nor good of your own accord; it is all God’s. There, the Alpha and Omega are crucified in the ✝ <term>Secret of Secrets</term><note>original: "Arcano Arcanorum"</note>; the earth and heaven are purified, and no creature could either walk, stand, live, nor hover without the Cross, or upon—

You have forgotten this, deceived by the serpent, such that you pay no heed to the <term>Secret Cross</term><note>original: "Arcanum Creutz"; likely referring to the mystical intersection of the divine and the mundane.</note>, which is the essence, life, and breath of all things.

When you do evil or good, do you think it comes from you, that you are capable of it yourself? By no means: That is the great Secret of Secrets in Heaven and Earth, and here is the meaning to which wisdom belongs; who is so wise, and who can interpret it?

This <note>original: "NB." (nota bene/note well)</note> place of God is the Hell and fiery pool of the damned, and it is a Heaven and gate of the blessed. ...can say anything truthfully that is not at the same time a lie; the reason is the <term>Secret Cross</term><note>original: "Creutz Arcanum"; referring to the mystical intersection of opposites symbolized by the cross.</note> itself, in which the End and the Beginning are always essentially one, and must not be separated.

The <term>Union of Opposites</term><note>Latin: "Combinatio Contradictoriorum"; the philosophical idea that truth is found in the synthesis of contradictory statements.</note> through the <term>Alpha</term><note>Greek: α</note> and <term>Omega</term><note>Greek: ω</note> gives the entire, perfect understanding and **perfect knowledge of all things**. If you take the End alone for yourself, without the Beginning (which is nonetheless impossible), you miss the † <term>Secret</term><note>original: "Arcani"; the author frequently uses the cross symbol to stand for the hidden divine mystery.</note> and are a **sectarian** and a **heretic**. But if you take the Beginning alone for yourself without the End, you remain a **sectarian** and a **heretic**, a deceiver and a seducer, for you miss the † Secret. However, if you take both together and unite Alpha and Omega—the End with the Beginning and the Beginning with the End—then you strike the † Secret and remain in the center point. You cannot err; you receive **perfect and absolute knowledge in all faculties and sciences**, and not without reason.

This † Secret is a wellspring, the origin of the **Trinity** and of all **creatures**. Just as everything has flowed out from it, so must everything be known and taught from it—namely, from the Alpha and Omega, which is from the **Union of Opposites**. The **rational logicians**<note>Latin: "rationales Dialectici"</note> can never grasp this unless they become **purely mental**<note>Latin: "Mentales"; referring to those whose understanding has been elevated to the spiritual mind.</note> and are raised above the **Light of Nature**<note>Latin: "Lumen Naturæ"; a term used in alchemy and mysticism to describe the natural intellect or knowledge derived from the physical world, which is limited compared to divine revelation.</note>, which is a subtle deception.

If a man could understand **Theology**, **that is, the Apocalypse**<note>Latin: "Theologiam, i. e. Apocalypsin"; implying that the study of God is synonymous with the Unveiling or Revelation.</note>, then God would not be God. Such speech is both right and wrong, true and a lie. to write; yet note this: I say, if a person could understand the **Apocalypse** <note>original: "Apocalypsin"; the Book of Revelation, often considered the most mysterious book of the Bible.</note>, then God would not be God. This is true, for the person would want to be God himself, to teach himself—which is against the Faith. That is to say, God would not be God within the person, just as He is not God in all the **world-scholars** <note>original: "Weltgelehrten"; a term used here disparagingly for those who rely on secular or intellectual learning rather than spiritual revelation.</note>, for their rational knowledge pushes GOD back; and that is also true.

God is God, and remains GOD in all, through all, and above all. For even if those world-scholars who have no God had never been born, God would still be God. But that such a person is without God, he witnesses himself by the fact that he considers himself to be God—believing he knows and understands **Theology** <note>original: "Theologiam"</note>, arts, and languages by himself, though he cannot even move without God—that is, without the **Secret of the Cross** <term>Secret of the Cross</term><note>original: "Creutz Arcanum"; the mystical intersection of divine and earthly reality.</note>. If God were his God, he would be a Christian and would bring the Beginning and the End together, recognizing himself as an unfit, unworthy tool, etc. Can a saw or an axe do anything without the carpenter? Nothing at all. Can clay do anything without the potter? Nothing at all; and much less can a human do anything without God, without the Cross. Even if he flees the **Secret of the Cross** along with **Satan**, he still has his power, spirit, breath, and life from Him, through Him. He is the Light and Life of the angels and devils of all creatures; **in him we live, move, and have our being** <note>original: "in ipso viuimus, mouemur & sumus, Actor. 17"; a reference to Acts 17:28.</note>. God knows and understands nothing: He understands and knows everything. The **creature** <note>original: "Creatur"; any created being, including humans and spirits.</note> knows and understands nothing: it knows and understands everything. In this speech, you find **Alpha** <note>Greek: $\alpha$</note> and **Omega** <note>Greek: $\omega$</note>—namely, the **Secret of the Cross**, through which all things are held together.

nor can they be led to it; for they cling to their **human reason** <term>human reason</term><note>original: "Vernunfft"; in this mystical tradition, this refers to the limited, earthly intellect that attempts to master divine mysteries through logic alone, rather than through spiritual surrender.</note>; they do not want to die <note>This refers to "mystical death," the process of letting go of the ego and self-will to be reborn in God.</note>, they want to teach themselves, they want to be God themselves; they believe they are the source and have no need for God in the matter. Everything depends on the understanding of the Beast and the number of his name, **666**. That is, on the knowledge of the **Trinity** <note>original: "Trinitet"</note>, both heavenly and earthly; for truly the earth cannot exist without the heaven, the temporal cannot exist without the eternal; neither is entirely for itself alone.

Two make a whole and a perfect thing; the visible cannot exist without the invisible; action cannot exist without rest, and rest is not without action; day is not without night; joy is not without sorrow; pleasure is not without pain; Heaven is not without Hell; these two must be one through the **Secret of the Cross** <term>Secret of the Cross</term><note>original: "Creutz Arcanum"; the hidden pivot point where the divine and the material, or the light and the dark, meet and are reconciled.</note>, for Hell stands just as much in the center of the **Secret of the Cross** <note>original: "Creutz Arcani"</note> as Heaven does; it is a summation and a container of Heaven and Hell, of the eternal and the temporal.

### 156.

<gloss>1</gloss> <gloss>2</gloss>
...[let] the Spirit and the Word, with their governance, be like the Sun, which contains the Day within itself. The Sun is assigned to the Spirit, representing the spiritual or heavenly life, which is a Light or a Day, and so forth.

The Word, however, is ascribed to the Moon, which contains the Night within itself and governs the earthly kingdom, which is sheer misery and darkness. Thus, these two—God and the Word—have the heavenly and earthly kingdoms to govern. Namely, as previously stated, God, who is the Spirit, governs the heavenly and divine kingdom; this kingdom only rules the Day and holds within itself the first "time" <note>original: "Zeitt"; likely referring to the "time, times, and half a time" from the prophetic books of Daniel and Revelation</note>.

The Son, as <image-desc>circular icon of a sun with a face</image-desc> the Person or Center of the Spirit and <image-desc>circular icon of an eight-pointed star</image-desc> the Word, possesses the Morning and the Evening. He contains the heavenly and earthly kingdoms as a total concept encompassing both, just as the Morning Star separates the Night from the Day, and the Evening Star separates the Day from the Night. Such a Star is a medium <note>original: "Mittel"; a middle point or mediator</note> of the heavenly and earthly kingdoms—that is, of the Day and the Night. Thus Christ, God and Man, is the Center given to us by God,
<sig>I</sig>

<gloss>2</gloss>
the true, revealed Cornerstone and Foundation of the Spirit and the Word. This Word—which is the Holy Spirit <note>The author here identifies the "Word" that governs the earthly realm with the Holy Spirit, a specific theological interpretation of how the Trinity interacts with the material world</note>—governs the earthly kingdom and is an heir to it. It holds the Night within it along with the Moon, <image-desc>circular icon of a crescent moon with a face</image-desc> which is compared to the Word and only shines at night. This Word contains the third "half-time."

### 157.

<sig>V iij</sig>

Thus, the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—that is, the Spirit, the Person, and the Word—now reveal themselves in a natural way.

Just as it is in the natural realm according to the outer person, so it is also in the divine realm, according to the heavenly persons. One should note profound matters here, all of which can be demonstrated naturally. Namely, as mentioned above: what the Spirit and the Word are to govern—that is, the Word that is present, and the Word that is God <note>original: "das Wort/ so da ist Gott"; a reference to John 1:1, "and the Word was God."</note>. Now, just as these two sink into the center and unite as one single God, in the same way, Day and Night, Sun and Moon, Fire and Water must harmonize with one another before Him and after the Crucifixion. To us natural men, this indeed seems foolish—that one could unite water with fire. Oh dear reader, let no one find this strange or unbelievable, for it is as clear as day, which could be proven through many examples, etc.

Thus, it is forever Day, and also forever Night; this Day and Night form a body <term>Corpus</term> <note>Latin: "body"; used here to describe the totality of existence as a single structure</note> in which the knowledge of all things can be explained naturally, as well as through Jesus Christ, without human interference. Now, when the time in which the Word currently rules—as if in the night with all creatures—shall return again into its nothingness, just as it was in the beginning:

Then eternity as God (which is now hidden because of the natural "Something" <note>original: "Ichts"; a term often used in German mysticism to represent "somethingness" or manifestation, the opposite of "Nichts" or nothingness</note> or the Night of the divine Nothingness) will once again be the Day and the eternal, single "Something." From this Sun, all natural or created stars—that is, time with its creations and creatures—will become nothing. For it is impossible in the bright day, when the sun is fully over the circle of the earth, to recognize any of the stars in the heaven because of its bright shine; only the Sun remains, or the Spirit, which is God, from whom all stars receive their life and light.

# Concerning the Revelation of Jesus Christ.

Dear Christian people: when Jesus Christ, God’s Son, is revealed by the Holy Spirit through his Father—just as the Father is revealed by the Son—then not only will the so-called Christians become [true] Christians, but all peoples—Gentiles <note>original: "Heyden"; at this time, a general term for non-Christians or pagans.</note>, Turks <note>original: "Türcken"; a historical term used broadly for Muslims, particularly those of the Ottoman Empire.</note>, Tatars <note>original: "Tattern"; historically referring to various nomadic Turkic and Mongol peoples of Central Asia.</note>, etc.—will accept the Christian faith and become Christian people. Up until now, as one can plainly see, Jesus Christ and the Christian faith have never truly been revealed, for the love of one's neighbor and the love of God are completely and utterly locked away. In their place, the "flesh" <note>original: "das Fleisch"; a biblical term for the lower, sinful human nature driven by worldly desires.</note> reigns—that is <note>original: "i.e." (id est)</note>: idolatry, fornication, drunkenness, envy, anger, quarreling, strife, murder, theft, injustice, and all excess and ambition. All of this is entirely against the revelation of Jesus Christ and against his new <insert>Birth</insert> creature <term>New Creature: a concept from the New Testament (2 Corinthians 5:17) describing a person who has been spiritually transformed and "reborn" through faith.</term>. Furthermore, why do we miserable humans accuse one another so that we are thus set against each other? Why indeed do we bite and quarrel? Has the love that was poured out through the Spirit then been completely extinguished? Does no one then respect another? Are we then irrational animals, such that one person tears apart, devours, and shames the other? Were heaven and earth created for only one person? Oh no! God, come soon and send us the revelation of Jesus Christ your Son; make the sheep united, you one and only Shepherd. Renew our hearts, minds, spirit, and thoughts, so that we—being no longer blind—do not condemn, kill, and murder one another.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>The author laments that true Christianity is currently hidden by human vice and conflict, arguing that a genuine revelation of Christ would unite all peoples, including Muslims and pagans, in universal love. He concludes with a prayer for spiritual renewal to end the "animal-like" violence among humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Revelation of Christ, Universalism, New Creature, Christian Unity, Pacifism, Spiritual Rebirth, Vices of the Flesh</keywords>

--- Page 292 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s passionate exhortation regarding the "Revelation of Jesus Christ." Having previously argued that true Christianity has been "locked away," the author now directly addresses the social hierarchies of the 17th century—kings, nobles, and scholars—accusing them of hypocrisy and pride. The catchword from the previous page, "aber" (but), connects to the ongoing critique of those who claim the name of Christ but act against his teachings.</meta>

167.

O you kings, potentates, and princes of this world: pray to GOD for the revelation of JESUS Christ, his beloved Son; then you will also know how to act differently toward your own heart and love—that is, toward your subjects. I say that you do not have even a tiny spark of love for your neighbor, much less do you love God. Do not boast of the Christian faith or of salvation in Jesus Christ, you who are called "Christian authorities" <term>Obrigkeit: a term for the governing powers or magistracy, often viewed in this period as ordained by God.</term> but who are, in your deeds, heathens and Turks <note>original: "Heyden vnd Türcken"; historical shorthand used to describe those outside the Christian faith, here used to accuse Christian rulers of acting like "unbelievers."</note>—complete enemies and not friends of Christ. If you wish to be friends of Christ, then accept his commandment and love your neighbor—not money, property, castles, wealth, cities, pride, etc. Love your neighbor; that alone remains eternal, for everything else passes away.

O you noble counts, knights, and noblemen: it is necessary that we pray to God the Creator for the revelation of Jesus Christ, his dear Son, who is mocked and despised and yet is the greatest. You, however, are great and highly esteemed, endowed with offices by your superiors; you despise everyone, and no one is considered your equal. The same is found among the scholars, who above all are considered highly learned; these are the greatest in the world, sent out to rule land and people because they have studied. Such "noble fellows" <note>original: "edle Knaben"; likely used here with heavy sarcasm to mock the arrogance of young or entitled scholars.</note>—they have sat with God in his council, and He has entrusted to them what He intends to do with his poor people of this world who are so full of contempt. Therefore, it is seemingly right that they rule and judge matters in this way, as if they have received an eye from GOD even in their "foot" <note>This likely refers to the "body politic" metaphor: the author sarcastically suggests these scholars believe even their lowest parts (the foot) possess divine vision (the eye).</note>.

It is Love, O dear people, that understands, knows, and can do all things, for GOD is Love. You citizens, officials, and regents of the cities and villages: if you live in friendliness and kindness with your fellow citizens and subjects, then your governance is pleasing to our LORD JESUS Christ. So

<summary>The author delivers a sharp social and spiritual critique, calling on rulers, nobles, and scholars to repent of their pride and material greed. He asserts that true Christian leadership is defined by love for one's neighbors and subjects, rather than through academic study or inherited status.</summary>
<keywords>social hierarchy, critique of authority, Christian love, hypocrisy, governance, equality, neighborly love</keywords>

--- Page 293 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s critique from page 167. Having previously lambasted rulers and nobles for their hypocrisy, the text now transitions into a universal call for spiritual renewal and a specific, sharp rebuke of the theologians and clergy of the day.</meta>

168.

but where **pride** <term>Hoffart: a term for haughty pride or arrogance, often considered the root of all other sins in Reformation-era theology.</term> reigns, we cannot know Christ. Nor should anyone boast of Him with their mouth, for He sees the heart; where love is, all injustices fall away in all things that are committed in the world.

All people in this world—rich and poor, man and woman, young and old, great and small—let us pray to God for the **revelation** of Jesus Christ, His Son. Then we shall live together as one, peaceful, friendly, patient, gentle, and humble of heart. We will become one heart—even with the Turks and Tatars <note>original: "Türcken vnd Tattern"; 17th-century shorthand used to represent the furthest reaches of the non-Christian world, indicating the author's radical vision of global spiritual unity.</note> and all the islands—one love, one community, one Church, of one faith and mind in Christ Jesus, through whom we are saved.

You theologians and clergy, along with all teachers: you ought to be taught by God. You must proclaim Jesus Christ of Nazareth, the crucified one, to all people with true knowledge and teaching. This must be done out of love, and not for the sake of money or profit—which is already an error found among you. You are almost like the night and the darkness that covers the whole land, and thus you darken the world through your **offense** <term>ärgernus: literally "stumbling block" or "scandal"; refers to behavior by religious leaders that causes others to lose faith.</term>. It is also necessary for you to pray to God the Almighty for the revelation of Jesus Christ, the Son of God and of the Virgin. Only then may you rightly lead the people as lights of this world. If you have this enlightenment from God, then through such knowledge, the quarreling among you—which is powerfully great and ruins many lands and people (because of **sectarianism** <term>Secterey: the division of the church into competing denominations or "sects," which the author views as a destruction of Christian unity.</term>) and still ruins them today—will be entirely abolished. Further, brotherly love and unity will be among you, with friendliness, of one mind, one knowledge, one faith, and one salvation, in entire and perfect love of one's neighbor and of God. Teach the people to know Christ Jesus, who is made for us our wisdom, our righteousness, our sanctification, and our redemption—so that, as it is written: *He who boasts, let him boast in the Lord.* (1 Corinthians 1).

<meta>Continuing from page 169, which concluded a mystical, ecstatic prayer to Divine Love, the author now shifts abruptly in tone. 170.

## Of the Whore and the Great Whoredom
## and the Abomination on Earth / which is the Mother and spiritual Babylon / as mentioned in the 17th chapter of the Apocalypse <term>Apocalypse: The Book of Revelation in the Bible. Chapter 17 describes a vision of a "Great Harlot" sitting on a scarlet beast, representing spiritual or worldly corruption.</term>.

Every and each creature <term>Creatur: In this historical context, "creature" refers to any created thing or being—humans, animals, or even material objects—distinguished from the Creator.</term> / that I have created from the beginning of the world / and that will yet be created / until the end of the world / has played the harlot / and still plays the harlot / until the end with this Whore / which is the spiritual Babylon / full of abomination and uncleanness / etc. But the mystery of this Whore is entirely hidden and unknown among us humans / so that among a thousand there is not one / who would consider / what kind of whore this might be / for everyone thinks / that a whore is only one / who sleeps with another / and they think that is enough / and that whoredom belongs only in this category <note>original: "Capitel"; while it literally means "chapter," here it refers to a specific topic or sin.</note> / and speaks only of harlots and adulterers.

Therefore, note well that it indeed takes its name from this / as when the Angel speaks / Come and see / I will show you the judgment of the great Whore / etc. What then is whoredom / or what does the word "Whore" mean? That is [called] a whore <note>original: "jch eine Hure"; likely a transcription error or archaic variant for "auch" (also) or "ja" (indeed), though it could read "I am a whore" if the author is speaking in a representative voice.</note> / who has committed whoredom with another (. for a virgin is not usually scolded as a whore / although according to this understanding she is one / and there is no human excluded.) <note>The author suggests a radical theological point: that spiritually, every human being is "unchaste" because they have turned their desires toward the world rather than God.</note>

Since that is called a whore / according to our understanding / which has committed whoredom with another / then note / that here there must be two / who wish to fulfill whoredom with one another. What then do both the whore and the whoremonger <term>Hurer: A male fornicator or one who frequents prostitutes.</term> usually begin with one another / namely internal and external—

<meta>catchword: Joy [Frewde]</meta>

<summary>The author begins a theological exposition on the "Whore of Babylon" from the Book of Revelation, arguing that whoredom is not merely a physical sin but a universal spiritual condition affecting all of creation. He asserts that every person is a "spiritual whore" insofar as they are joined to the world rather than to God.</summary>
<keywords>Whore of Babylon, Apocalypse, spiritual whoredom, creature, Babylon, Revelation 17, sin, idolatry</keywords>

joy and fleshly lust / also with such desire / that they are weakened in their highest inward powers / once the deed is accomplished / there whoredom is committed / and for that reason they are called whoremongers / and they know / that it is against **GOD'S** ten commandments / and that **GOD** does not leave such sin unpunished / etc. In this way and no other I now say specifically of all people and creatures / that they drive at nothing else nor less than harlotry and whoredom / just as whoremongers bring together great desire and passion / and commit their lust with one another / and fulfill the same / so too do all creatures play the harlot with this Whore and spiritual Babylon. Therefore, dear person, consider and see first of all the spiritual estate <term>spiritual estate: the clergy or religious leadership of the time</term> / how they play the harlot and tend to their lust / and <margin>Reader's marks in ink (three curved brackets)</margin> have it in themselves / that they let themselves be called Masters and Rabbis of **SCRIPTURE** / and value and hold themselves more worthy than **CHRIST** considered Himself / yet this is not regarded as whoredom or sin in the world / I say however / (NB.) <note>original: "NB." for *nota bene*, meaning "note well."</note> that [physical] whoredom is more tolerable than this. What should one now say of kings / princes and **LORDS** / since then all play the harlot with this Whore / how heartily and willfully they behave in all their intentions / deeds and omissions: In short / if one were to write down all their failings and pleasures / no book of paper would suffice. 
All this pleasure of theirs now / which they commit with clothing / eating and drinking / hunting / riding / shooting / is pure whoredom / and **GOD** will not leave any of it unpunished.
